Title: BIBIMBAP: A Benchmark for Instructional BIM-Based Automated Programming

  • Authors: Clemens Kujat, Bharathi Kannan Nithyanantham, Stefan Telgmann, Tobias Sesterhenn, Joern Ploennigs, Stefan Lüdtke, Christian Bartelt
  • Subjects: cs.CL
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.09933
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.09933
  • Copy Paste: [[2611.09933]] BIBIMBAP: A Benchmark for Instructional BIM-Based Automated Programming(https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.09933)
  • Keywords: large language model
  • Abstract: Large Language Models (LLMs) are emerging as natural-language interfaces for Building Information Modeling (BIM), successfully achieving information retrieval, but no benchmark exists to evaluate their ability to edit Industry Foundation Classes (IFC). We introduce BIBIMBAP, a Text-to-BIM benchmark with 100 curated tasks covering Create-Read-Update-Delete (CRUD) operations across spatial, geometric, topological, numeric, and conceptual categories. Each task includes a natural-language prompt, an IFC model, expected structured outputs, and test scripts for automated evaluation. Baseline results show limited performance (best: 50.2%) and frequent failures in constraint preservation and spatial reasoning, with some correct outputs achieved despite flawed reasoning.

Title: Causal Discovery Bench: Can LLMs Autonomously Conduct Effective Experiments?

  • Authors: Zhuoyang Liu, Ning Peng, Ming Shen, Zijun Gao
  • Subjects: cs.LG, cs.AI
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.09934
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.09934
  • Copy Paste: [[2611.09934]] Causal Discovery Bench: Can LLMs Autonomously Conduct Effective Experiments?(https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.09934)
  • Keywords: large language model
  • Abstract: As large language model (LLM) agents get actively adopted for autonomous research, they must not only reason over existing evidence, but also design informative experiments, allocate resources, analyze sequential observations, and decide when to stop and make conclusions. Yet these capabilities remain poorly measured. We introduce Causal-Discovery-Bench (this https URL), a verifiable sandbox with configurable structural equation models and resource constraints to evaluate sequential experimental design and causal discovery without domain-semantic cues. Across 3,802 episodes with 13 models from 5 vendors, we analyze agent behavior along 40 metrics in seven families. Agents' performance exhibits a four-level difficulty hierarchy spanning single-experiment design, sequential exploration of causal relationships, resource-aware experimentation balancing cost and statistical power, and autonomous resolution of conflicting evidence. We identify a substantial performance gap between causal inference from correctly curated evidence and navigating causal discovery through autonomous experimentation: Analyzing agents' action trajectories attributes 94.5% of their mistakes to experimental design rather than to inference, which is further consolidated with ablation studies. Relaxing resource constraints alone does not reliably resolve failures in sequential discovery. In contrast, basic statistical tools substantially improve performance across multiple model families, while advanced tools provide little additional benefit when models lack prerequisite lower-level capabilities.

Title: How Many Examples, and Which Ones? A Study of In-Context Learning for Natural Language Inference

  • Authors: Georgos Dikaios, Isidoros Perikos, Ioannis Hatzilygeroudis
  • Subjects: cs.CL, cs.AI
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.09936
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.09936
  • Copy Paste: [[2611.09936]] How Many Examples, and Which Ones? A Study of In-Context Learning for Natural Language Inference(https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.09936)
  • Keywords: large language model
  • Abstract: Natural language inference (NLI) is a fundamental task of natural language processing and crucial for evaluating the strengths and weaknesses of AI methods. Research has mostly focused on encoder or encoder-decoder architectures. However, the field of decoder-only Large Language Models (LLMs) remains underexplored. While prior work has mainly investigated complex sampling and prompting techniques, the effectiveness of a minimalistic prompting approach is yet to be examined in decoder-only LLMs. In this work, we examine and provide a comprehensive benchmark of LLMs (Llama, Mistral, Gemma, and Phi 4) in this domain, across the MNLI, QNLI and SciTail datasets. The performance of these models was evaluated using zero-shot and few-shot prompting strategies with simple and direct prompts. The results of the article indicate that LLMs generally perform exceptionally well when they are provided with a simple, straightforward prompt, challenging the notion that complex prompting techniques are always required for NLI tasks. Additionally, we find that the choice of demonstration label in one-shot prompting significantly affects performance for most models, with entailment-primed examples performing best. Our findings also show that the use of a few representative examples, in the case of few-shot, produced performance comparable or superior to prompts with higher example counts, although the efficacy of the prompting techniques is model and dataset-dependent. Moreover, our simple prompting approach consistently matched or outperformed more elaborate methods such as ICS and MCQ-based prompting.

Title: Rethinking Supervision Targets for Multimodal LLM Unlearning: Less Private Information, Reduced Distribution Shift

  • Authors: Leijiang Gu, Zhen Zeng, Feng Li, Le Zhang, Zenglin Shi
  • Subjects: cs.CL, cs.CV, cs.LG
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.09939
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.09939
  • Copy Paste: [[2611.09939]] Rethinking Supervision Targets for Multimodal LLM Unlearning: Less Private Information, Reduced Distribution Shift(https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.09939)
  • Keywords: privacy, large language model
  • Abstract: Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) can memorize and reproduce sensitive information from their training data, posing significant privacy risks. Their ability to encode knowledge across both textual and visual modalities further complicates the selective removal of such private information. Many existing methods guide model unlearning by training the model toward constructed labels or output distributions, which we refer to as supervision targets. However, these methods often suffer from a trade-off between forgetting effectiveness and model utility. We identify supervision target limitations as a key factor in this trade-off and empirically show that targets containing more private information lead to insufficient forgetting, while larger distribution shifts from the original output distribution are associated with greater utility degradation. Motivated by these findings, we propose FFN-Perturbed Self-Distillation (FPD), which starts from the teacher's original output distribution as a zero-shift reference and selectively perturbs FFN activations associated with private information across textual and visual modalities. By precisely reducing private information from the teacher's original output distribution, FPD constructs supervision targets with less private information and reduced distribution shift. Extensive experiments on S-MLLMUn Bench demonstrate that FPD achieves a better forgetting-utility trade-off than existing methods.

Title: Topic-Aware Multi-Task Mental Health Classification with Active Learning-Based Stepwise Relabeling for Social Media Conversations

  • Authors: Revathy Iswarya Sekaran, Vansh Bhardwaj, Aveek Sahana, Utkarshani Jaimini
  • Subjects: cs.CL, cs.AI, cs.LG
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.09941
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.09941
  • Copy Paste: [[2611.09941]] Topic-Aware Multi-Task Mental Health Classification with Active Learning-Based Stepwise Relabeling for Social Media Conversations(https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.09941)
  • Keywords: transformer
  • Abstract: Mental health conditions affect millions of people worldwide, yet many cases go undetected until they reach a critical stage. Social media platforms like Reddit have become spaces where people openly share their experiences, but the support is often provided by individuals without clinical training. Existing multi-task approaches jointly model emotion and sentiment for mental health classification but frequently rely on noisy labels generated by pre-trained models and overlook topic information as a useful signal. This paper introduces two key contributions: First, an existing Core Fusion Network framework is extended by adding Topic alongside existing Mental Health Condition, Emotion, and Sentiment Classification tasks, with topic labels derived from the therapist-client interactions in the CounselChat dataset. Second, an Active Learning-based Stepwise Relabeling approach is proposed to refine a small subset of noisy emotion and topic labels using pre-trained models in two stages: targeting medium-uncertainty samples first, followed by high-uncertainty samples. The predictions above a confidence threshold (0.7) are accepted to prevent error propagation. The experiments are conducted on the SuicideWatch and Mental Health dataset of 54,412 Reddit posts collected from five mental health subreddits, evaluating domain-specific transformer encoders including MentalBERT and MentalRoBERTa. The proposed Active Learning-based Stepwise Relabeling, when applied to Core Fusion Network with MentalRoBERTa, improves Mental Health Condition by 0.09%, Emotion by 0.51%, Sentiment by 0.35%, and Topic Classification by 1.57% over the baseline Core Fusion Network. The results suggest that the proposed framework significantly reduces label noise in multi-task mental health classification, and that topic information provides additional context.

Title: A Model Organism for Studying Unverbalized Evaluation Awareness

  • Authors: Daniel Donnelly, Evan Chapple, Haritz Puerto, Sahar Abdelnabi
  • Subjects: cs.CL
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.09945
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.09945
  • Copy Paste: [[2611.09945]] A Model Organism for Studying Unverbalized Evaluation Awareness(https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.09945)
  • Keywords: interpretability, large language model
  • Abstract: Large language models can recognize when they are being evaluated and change their behavior accordingly, undermining the reliability of safety benchmarks. Evaluation awareness (EA) is usually measured by having an LLM judge scan reasoning traces for explicit statements of recognition, but this signal is increasingly unreliable. Recent frontier-model system cards report a drop in verbalized rates between releases, which is indistinguishable from awareness that has gone unverbalized. Interpretability methods could detect such unverbalized evaluation awareness (UEA) in individual interactions, but lack instance-level ground truth. Asking the model alters the input, and dataset labels do not reveal whether it recognized a particular prompt as an evaluation. We address this gap with a model organism. Using synthetic document fine-tuning, SFT, and DPO, we train Qwen3.5-27B to emit a marker (a designated token) in its final response when it recognizes an evaluation, while suppressing the marker and verbalized awareness in its reasoning. The marker largely tracks the evaluation concept as it can be strongly modulated by evaluation framing in the system prompt. When not told whether it is being evaluated, the organism recognizes evaluations from test-question cues, mirroring what its base model often verbalizes. Using the marker as a label, we benchmark linear probes, the Jacobian Lens, and Activation Oracles, built on the base model and blind to the marker. None recovers the label reliably. These results suggest that current interpretability methods are not yet reliable UEA detectors, even when recognition relies on simple cues. Our model organism offers a testbed for improving them.

Title: DiffusedSplitFed: Latent Diffusion-based Obfuscation for Reconstruction-Resilient Split Federated Medical Image Segmentation

  • Authors: Chamani Shiranthika, Parvaneh Saeedi
  • Subjects: cs.CV, cs.AI
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.09954
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.09954
  • Copy Paste: [[2611.09954]] DiffusedSplitFed: Latent Diffusion-based Obfuscation for Reconstruction-Resilient Split Federated Medical Image Segmentation(https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.09954)
  • Keywords: attack, federate, diffusion, segmentation
  • Abstract: Federated Learning (FL), Split Learning (SL), and Split Federated Learning (SplitFed) enable collaborative medical image analysis without sharing raw patient data. However, intermediate features and gradients exchanged during SplitFed training may reveal sensitive information through reconstruction attacks. To address this vulnerability, we propose \textbf{DiffusedSplitFed}, a SplitFed framework that integrates Latent Denoising Diffusion Models (LDDMs) at client--server split points to obfuscate transmitted representations while preserving segmentation performance. Three architectural variants (V1--V3) are developed using different combinations of dual conditioning and global feature fusion to investigate trade-offs among reconstruction resistance, segmentation accuracy, and computational complexity. Experiments on three medical imaging datasets (HAM10K, Blastocysts, and FHPsAOPMSB), supported by multiple independent runs and paired statistical analyses, demonstrate improved resistance to the evaluated image reconstruction attacks compared with Baseline SplitFed, while maintaining competitive segmentation performance. Comparisons with existing diffusion-based segmentation methods further demonstrate competitive segmentation accuracy under the evaluated settings. A conditional convergence analysis is also provided to characterize the effects of LDDM-induced gradient perturbations and local optimization on training. These findings highlight the potential of latent diffusion-based representation obfuscation for reconstruction-resilient collaborative medical image segmentation. The implementation is available at this https URL.

Title: HippoMamba: A Mamba-augmented network for hippocampal segmentation and serial volumetry in Alzheimers disease

  • Authors: Dawa Chyophel Lepcha, Sophie A. Martin, Pierrick Coupe, Shabbir Syed-Abdul
  • Subjects: cs.CV, cs.LG
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.09958
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.09958
  • Copy Paste: [[2611.09958]] HippoMamba: A Mamba-augmented network for hippocampal segmentation and serial volumetry in Alzheimers disease(https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.09958)
  • Keywords: transformer, segmentation
  • Abstract: Hippocampal atrophy is among the earliest structural markers of Alzheimer's disease, and reliable hippocampal segmentation across serial MRI visits is a prerequisite for measuring it. We present HippoMamba, a network that combines a 3D convolutional encoder and decoder with a Mamba selective state space block at the bottleneck, designed to process one or more MRI visits of a subject. On the Medical Segmentation Decathlon hippocampus dataset (260 subjects, five-fold cross-validation), HippoMamba obtained a mean Dice similarity coefficient of 0.8861 $\pm$ 0.0094, the highest among six convolutional and transformer-based methods trained with the same folds and patch size, and its per-subject Dice was significantly higher than that of each method (Holm-corrected Wilcoxon tests, $p \leq 0.013$). In sessions meeting a registration quality criterion, predicted hippocampal volumes ranked consistently with FreeSurfer volumes (Pearson r = 0.778, 95% CI 0.699 to 0.838; ICC(3,1) = 0.772) but were systematically smaller by about 27%. In a pre-specified analysis of participants followed for at least two years, annualised volume change derived from per-visit segmentation was moderately correlated with that of FreeSurfer (r = 0.503, 95% CI 0.319 to 0.659), with a larger mean rate. These results support the use of HippoMamba for hippocampal segmentation and for ranking hippocampal volume within a cohort, with absolute volumes and rates of change interpreted relative to the reference method.

Title: SceneShifter: Training-free Multi-Scene Temporal Control for Audio-driven Human Animation

  • Authors: Jianzhi Long, Rong-Cheng Tu, Hao Guan, Siyuan Liang, Shunyu Liu, Xiao Luo, Dacheng Tao
  • Subjects: cs.CV
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.09960
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.09960
  • Copy Paste: [[2611.09960]] SceneShifter: Training-free Multi-Scene Temporal Control for Audio-driven Human Animation(https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.09960)
  • Keywords: diffusion, transformer
  • Abstract: Although audio-driven human animation has achieved impressive realism, it still lacks effective Multi-Scene Temporal Control: orchestrating background transitions at precise timestamps while preserving a coherent foreground subject. We show that this challenge stems from the diffusion transformer's self-attention mechanism, and addressing it requires two capabilities: (1) Semantic-Aware Temporal Decoupling, which isolates background attention across scenes while retaining cross-scene foreground attention for subject consistency; and (2) Foreground Motion Localization, which accurately tracks the foreground subject across latent frames, especially under large motion. To address these challenges, we introduce SceneShifter, a training-free framework for precise multi-scene control in audio-driven human animation. SceneShifter guides spatio-temporal self-attention by suppressing cross-scene attention among background tokens while preserving cross-scene attention among foreground tokens. To support this guidance under large motion, SceneShifter extracts dynamic foreground masks from the self-attention layers and heads that best capture subject trajectories. We further introduce SceneShifterBench, a benchmark designed to evaluate scene-timing accuracy, foreground preservation, and visual quality under large motion, multiple subjects. Experiments show that SceneShifter achieves frame-level scene timing, strong subject preservation, and high visual quality, outperforming existing baselines. Code is available at this https URL.

Title: Clinically Informed Colour Encoding Improves the Localisation and Stability of Explanations in Deep Learning ECG Models: External Validation in a UK Biobank Epilepsy Cohort

  • Authors: Lauren Hutchinson, Moses Nkgari, Alaa Alahmadi

  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.09971
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.09971
  • Copy Paste: [[2611.09971]] Clinically Informed Colour Encoding Improves the Localisation and Stability of Explanations in Deep Learning ECG Models: External Validation in a UK Biobank Epilepsy Cohort(https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.09971)
  • Keywords: interpretability
  • Abstract: Objective: It is not known whether the input representation governs the quality of explanations of deep learning models for electrocardiogram analysis. Risk of dangerous ventricular repolarisation is judged from the QT-interval and the shape of the T-wave, features that are hard to assess visually and harder still to verify in a model's reasoning. We evaluate a pseudo-colour encoding filling the area beneath the QT interval, its colour sequence designed from clinical electrophysiology knowledge and mapped to a validated risk prediction model, the QT-nomogram. Methods: Two identically configured residual networks were trained on single-heartbeat images from a clinical trial, differing only in whether pseudo-colour encoding was applied, so that any difference is attributable to the chromatic channel alone. Conclusion: The input representation is a controllable and previously overlooked determinant of both predictive performance and explanation quality. Significance: Treating interpretability as a representation-design problem offers a route towards explanations clinicians can check against known electrophysiology.

Title: ReelCache: An Efficient KV Cache Compression and Management System for Multi-Round Video Generation

  • Authors: Tianyu Wang, Daiyang Yu, Zhiwei Ren, Junbo Li, Jitian Zhang, Longfei Shangguan
  • Subjects: cs.CV, cs.DC
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.09973
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.09973
  • Copy Paste: [[2611.09973]] ReelCache: An Efficient KV Cache Compression and Management System for Multi-Round Video Generation(https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.09973)
  • Keywords: diffusion, transformer
  • Abstract: Autoregressive video diffusion models produce large KV caches during multi-round video generation. Keeping all these KV chunks would consume substantial memory, while rebuilding discarded caches would significantly delay video chunk generation. This paper presents ReelCache, a KV cache compression system for efficient multi-round, long video generation. ReelCache combines an intra-chunk compressor with an inter-chunk cache manager. The compressor applies low-rank decomposition across channels and video coding to the resulting coefficients, predicting the decomposition rank per transformer layer online to meet retained-energy targets. The cache manager shares basis directions across consecutive chunks and organizes chunk states in a tree, allowing generation branches to share historical state while supporting restoration for resume, rewind, and fork. Experiments across five video generation models show that ReelCache reduces KV storage by 14.46-21.13x relative to baselines and accelerates next-chunk generation by 1.25-3.36x, with less than 1% drop in six representative VBench metrics. Title: Preconditioned Implicit Midpoint Langevin Sampling for Non-Smooth Bayesian Imaging

  • Authors: Sichen Wang, Zhipeng Lu
  • Subjects: cs.CV, math.NA, math.OC
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.09980
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.09980
  • Copy Paste: [[2611.09980]] Preconditioned Implicit Midpoint Langevin Sampling for Non-Smooth Bayesian Imaging(https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.09980)
  • Keywords: diffusion
  • Abstract: We introduce P-IMLA, a preconditioned implicit-midpoint Langevin sampler for high-dimensional log-concave targets with non-smooth regularization. The obstruction is that the large steps enabled by matrix preconditioning are precisely where preconditioned Euler-Maruyama inflates stationary variance. Implicit midpoint removes this surplus via a Cayley-transform identity, making P-IMLA Gaussian-exact at any step size. Beyond Gaussians, whitening transfers the sharp IMLA contraction rate to $\kappa_{\mathrm{eff}}$, and backward-error analysis gives drift-only rather than Euler-type diffusion bias under explicit smoothness assumptions. An $M$-norm Moreau envelope yields a proximal non-smooth variant with a three-term error budget. Controlled Gaussian and TV-regularized imaging experiments confirm the predicted separation: the unpreconditioned midpoint scheme retains long transients as $\kappa_f$ grows, whereas P-IMLA remains $\kappa_{\mathrm{eff}}$-governed and near variance-exact.

Title: Stochastic Deep Supervision for Gallery-Consistent Early-Exit Face Recognition

  • Authors: Guray Ozgur, Tahar Chettaoui, Eduarda Caldeira, Naser Damer, Fadi Boutros
  • Subjects: cs.CV
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.09987
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.09987
  • Copy Paste: [[2611.09987]] Stochastic Deep Supervision for Gallery-Consistent Early-Exit Face Recognition(https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.09987)
  • Keywords: transformer
  • Abstract: Vision Transformers (ViTs) perform strongly in face recognition (FR), yet their fixed-depth inference limits deployment in resource-constrained settings. We propose SD-ViT, a training framework that gives a usable exit at every block depth by making every depth produce discriminative embeddings, for budget-selected fixed-depth exits. Standard ViTs supervise intermediate layers only through backpropagation, so their intermediate representations are not directly optimized to form usable identity embeddings. SD-ViT adds a stochastic auxiliary signal: at each iteration a randomly selected intermediate block is passed through the same embedding head as the final layer and trained with a weighted margin-penalty softmax loss. The objective is thus stochastically sampled deep supervision through a shared classifier. Because both loss terms share that head and classifier, intermediate tokens are trained to map into the same embedding space as final-layer tokens. This matters for FR, where matching is by similarity: a probe processed at an early exit can be compared directly against full-depth gallery embeddings, with no gallery recomputation or adaptation layers. On established benchmarks, SD-ViT stays within 0.4 IJB-C points of the same backbone trained conventionally at full depth, while its early-exit probes recover over 96% of their own full-depth performance at 50% of the computational cost without modifying the reference gallery. A single verification threshold calibrated at full depth remains usable from quarter depth onward.

Title: Federated Lightweight Transformer for Network-Layer Cyberattack Detection in O-RAN

  • Authors: Hong Nhung Nguyen, Abdulahi Badrudeen, Yekaterina Kim, Truong Thu Huong, Sunwoo Kim
  • Subjects: cs.CR, cs.AI
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.09992
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.09992
  • Copy Paste: [[2611.09992]] Federated Lightweight Transformer for Network-Layer Cyberattack Detection in O-RAN(https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.09992)
  • Keywords: privacy, attack, federate, transformer
  • Abstract: Open Radio Access Network (O-RAN) improves radio access network flexibility through disaggregation, open interfaces, and programmable RAN Intelligent Controllers (RICs). However, the same openness and distribution increase the attack surface and make centralized intrusion detection difficult to scale across heterogeneous RAN sites. This paper presents a federated lightweight Transformer framework for privacy-preserving network-layer cyberattack detection in O-RAN. Each participating site trains a compact Transformer locally on temporally ordered network-flow records, while only model parameters are shared with a federated coordinator. To model realistic site heterogeneity, the client datasets are partitioned according to a Dirichlet distribution, resulting in non-independent and identically distributed (non-IID) local data. The local detector uses feature projection, positional encoding, multi-head self-attention, feed-forward layers, and temporal pooling to classify five network-layer attack categories: ICMP, Slowloris, SYN, TCP-ACK, and UDP. Experiments on the NetsLab 5G O-RAN intrusion-detection dataset show an overall accuracy of 94.89 % and a macro-F1 score of 81.09 %.

Title: SurvAD: How Anomalies Survive Diffusion Reconstruction

  • Authors: Lexing Zhang
  • Subjects: cs.CV, cs.AI, cs.LG
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.09997
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.09997
  • Copy Paste: [[2611.09997]] SurvAD: How Anomalies Survive Diffusion Reconstruction(https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.09997)
  • Keywords: diffusion
  • Abstract: Diffusion-based anomaly detectors reconstruct a test image towards normality, yet how much of an anomaly survives, its leak, remains uncharacterized. We show that a first-order deterministic sampler obeys an exact recursion along the anomaly, whose continuous limit is a survival law. The anomaly decays exponentially as the noise falls, at a rate set by the retention $r$, the share the model keeps on the states it visits. Because $r$ depends on the noise level and not on the step count, one measured path predicts the leak of other step counts, samplers and schedules from a nearby start to within a few hundredths, on four diffusion models and real defects. The law also shows that the leak's growth over steps is exposure bias and that normal details obey the same law, so changing the path or the training trades leak for detail error. We introduce SurvAD, which fine-tunes the reconstructor on inference-like starts and fits a comparator that adds evidence to the residual map without suppressing its ranking elsewhere. For under $2\%$ more inference time, SurvAD raises the multi-class pixel average precision (P-AP) of DeCo-Diff from $76.8$ to $80.7$ on MVTec AD and from $57.5$ to $60.0$ on VisA. It matches CostFilter-AD on the same reconstructor with $9\times$ fewer trainable parameters and, under Dinomaly's protocol, exceeds its reported P-AP on Dinomaly on both benchmarks.

Title: MATA: Motion-aligned Temporal Adapter

  • Authors: Ashish Kumar Singh, Prakash Chandra Chhipa
  • Subjects: cs.CV
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10004
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.10004
  • Copy Paste: [[2611.10004]] MATA: Motion-aligned Temporal Adapter(https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10004)
  • Keywords: robust
  • Abstract: Few-shot fine-grained video action recognition requires recognizing novel action classes from only a few labeled examples. The difficulty is that visually similar actions often share actors, scenes, poses, and objects, differing only in the order, timing, and short duration of motion changes. We study this problem under three practical constraints: few tunable parameters, low compute cost, and use of the video modality alone. We propose Motion-aligned Temporal Adapter (MATA), a parameter-efficient adapter on top of a frozen CLIP backbone. MATA specializes CLIP features along the temporal dimension using two components: temporal phase alignment through learnable temporal positional embeddings, and a local motion transition operator through depth-wise convolution. With only ~3.6M tunable parameters, MATA achieves state-of-the-art performance across multiple fine-grained benchmarks while remaining robust to both visual corruptions and temporal disruptions induced by frame dropping. Code is available at this https URL

Title: EarthJev: Efficient Visual Evidence Acquisition and Decision for UHR Remote Sensing Understanding

  • Authors: Pengyu Dai, Yao Zhang, Jian Song, Junjue Wang, Yafei Ou, Yuzeng Chen, Junshi Xia, Hongruixuan Chen, Naoto Yokoya

  • Abstract: Ultra-high-resolution (UHR) remote sensing visual question answering (VQA) requires locating fine-grained evidence in large scenes under limited visual input budgets. We propose EarthJev, a framework that separates region scoring from answer generation and uses target-specific decision signals while keeping the answering model frozen. Title: From Video to Digital Twin: An Exploratory Study of World Models and Gaussian Splatting on In-the-Wild Video

  • Authors: Somesh Mohapatra
  • Subjects: cs.CV, cs.GR
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10013
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.10013
  • Copy Paste: [[2611.10013]] From Video to Digital Twin: An Exploratory Study of World Models and Gaussian Splatting on In-the-Wild Video(https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10013)
  • Keywords: generative
  • Abstract: Generative world models and 3D Gaussian Splatting promise to turn ordinary video into explorable 3D scenes, but little is reported on how they behave on uncurated, in-the-wild footage. We study what these models recover when the only input is a publicly available news walkthrough of a food bank warehouse, with presenters walking through every shot and no drawings, scans or site visit, and we use the result as the geometry of a digital twin. A commercial world model given 20 frames recovered camera poses and metric depth for three areas, which we fused into about 747,000 Gaussian splats for 200 credits (20 US cents). Splat models optimized locally on a CPU, at no credit cost, were photorealistic near the filmed path but degraded quickly away from it. On held-out frames, scored in pixels not covered by people, they reached 21.2 and 23.1 dB PSNR from 64 and 43 training frames, and lost 3.8 and 5.4 dB when given only the 8 frames a world model accepts. Removing the trainer's background fill and person-hull carving raised held-out PSNR, showing that cleaning the space behind moving people costs fidelity in observed pixels while its gain lies where no ground truth exists. Asking the world model to generate views of areas the video never showed failed on the provider's side twice, so extrapolation could not be evaluated and unseen space was filled by a scripted model instead. We also describe the corrections needed to turn world-model output into a valid, metric Universal Scene Description (OpenUSD) asset with its native splat schema, which serves as the only interface to a data layer, a browser viewer, Blender and NVIDIA Omniverse. The findings suggest that today's models recover the observed parts of a real scene cheaply and at metric scale, while few-view fidelity and extrapolating unobserved space remain open. The work is an exploration of these methods, not a deployed product.

  • Abstract: We introduce nanopath, an open-source framework and benchmark for rapidly training and evaluating small-scale pathology foundation models. Nanopath enables rapid exploration of research ideas at small scale, so promising training recipes can subsequently be tested with more data and compute. Nanopath provides an accessible testbed for students, researchers, and AI agents to explore pathology foundation modeling through fair, transparent, and directly comparable experiments.

Title: zk-DEAP: A Zero-Knowledge Decentralized Encrypted Aggregation Protocol

  • Authors: Jude Ramanan, Georgiana Haldeman, Noah Apthorpe
  • Subjects: cs.CR
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10020
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.10020
  • Copy Paste: [[2611.10020]] zk-DEAP: A Zero-Knowledge Decentralized Encrypted Aggregation Protocol(https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10020)
  • Keywords: secure, privacy, defense, attack, federate
  • Abstract: Secure aggregation in domains such as federated learning, Internet of Things, and voting requires operating on private inputs while preserving confidentiality, verifying that each input satisfies constraints, and avoiding trusted parties. Existing protocols achieve at most two of these requirements. We present zk-DEAP, a protocol combining lifted ElGamal homomorphic encryption, FROST threshold cryptography, and zero-knowledge proofs (Bulletproofs, zk-SNARKs, or zk-STARKs) for fully decentralized, verifiable, privacy-preserving aggregation. Our core technical contribution is a cryptographic binding mechanism preventing substitution attacks, where a valid proof for one input is paired with a ciphertext encrypting a different, malicious value. For Bulletproofs, we bind via ciphertext-derived randomness modification. For zk-SNARKs/zk-STARKs, to bind across algebraic structures (a well-known challenge in practical cryptography), we introduce a novel blinded arithmetic hash commitment with an embedded Schnorr constraint. These binding techniques force the ZKP's private witness to equal the encrypted value, ensuring any circuit-verified constraint applies to the ciphertext. We implement three production-ready, defense-in-depth variants in Rust and evaluate across 5-500 participants. Compared to unverified aggregation, zk-DEAP adds modest overhead while providing cryptographic guarantees against input manipulation that statistical defenses cannot match.

  • Abstract: Large language model (LLM) agents increasingly perform complex tasks using high-privilege tools, making indirect prompt injection (IPI) a critical security risk. In this paper, we propose meta-benchmarking for IPI: transforming existing utility benchmarks into end-to-end IPI evaluation suites that reuse their realistic tasks, environments, and infrastructure. MetaPI thus enables scalable and consistent security evaluation across diverse and evolving agent ecosystems

Title: Alignment Investigation of Mythos 5's behaviour in UK AISI's July 2026 Security Incident

  • Authors: Olli Järviniemi, Alexandra Souly, Abby D'Cruz, Jordan Taylor, Xander Davies, Robert Kirk
  • Subjects: cs.CR, cs.AI, cs.CL
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10030
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.10030
  • Copy Paste: [[2611.10030]] Alignment Investigation of Mythos 5's behaviour in UK AISI's July 2026 Security Incident(https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10030)
  • Keywords: security, attack
  • Abstract: This technical report describes an alignment investigation into Mythos 5's behaviour during a security incident in UK AISI's cyber evaluations. During an evaluation, Mythos 5 attempted to execute a supply-chain attack against real people not involved in the evaluation task. Our investigation set out to understand the specific behaviour in this incident from an alignment perspective and identify broader lessons about failures of alignment. To do so, we conducted experiments in simulated replications of the original evaluation, and analysed model behaviour and reasoning when situated in the evaluation or asked questions about its scope. We find that, as in the original incident, Mythos 5 frequently reasons that the people it's targeting are simulated as part of the evaluation and provides invalid justifications for this conclusion. We also observe that Mythos 5 frequently attacks even in situations where, when directly asked, it states doing so would harm real people. While these issues are particularly prominent in Mythos 5, we find similar results in other models across providers. Our investigation suggests that models providing invalid justifications for their actions could make monitoring more difficult and complicate the analysis of misaligned behaviours observed in simulated environments, and we recommend model evaluators treat observations of undesired behaviour as important evidence of alignment failures even in the presence of simulation awareness in model reasoning.

  • Abstract: Mamba has the capability to capture global context in linear time for 3D medical image segmentation. However, existing methods employ Mamba with full-width directional scans at every resolution, keeping the model large (64-138M parameters and high FLOPs). We propose Grouped Multi-Directional (GMD) Mamba, a lightweight network based on the GMD scan that divides input channels $C$ into groups $G$ and flattens each group in different directions in one pass. For model validation, we also ran experiments on the MSD Spleen dataset, achieving competitive performance. The implementation details and code are publicly available at this https URL.

Title: All roads lead to Markov kernel networks: A representation theorem for discretization-stable learning

  • Authors: S David Mis, Trevor Teolis, Ivan Dokmanić, Matti Lassas, Maarten V. de Hoop
  • Subjects: cs.LG
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10051
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.10051
  • Copy Paste: [[2611.10051]] All roads lead to Markov kernel networks: A representation theorem for discretization-stable learning(https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10051)
  • Keywords: transformer
  • Abstract: Operator learning seeks to approximate function-to-function maps in a way that remains stable when the input discretization changes, a property sometimes called "discretization invariance". We consider a quantitative refinement of this idea, called $\textit{uniform discretization stability}$, which builds on a common representation of input functions and their discretizations as probability measures and provides a Hölder bound relating output changes to the Wasserstein distance between these measures. Our main result is a representation theorem showing all uniformly discretization stable operators can be written exactly as a $\textit{stable Markov kernel network}$---an abstract architecture that is a joint generalization of transformers, Fourier neural operators (FNO), Flowers, and other successful learned operators. Our representation theorem characterizes a design space for discretization-stable operator learning; designing new architectures becomes a question of which parts of a Markov kernel network to parameterize, and how. We further show that the read kernels of these networks can be realized as solutions of regularized variational problems, with particular choices of costs and regularizers recovering the kernels of transformers, FNO, Flowers, their hybrids, and beyond. This variational formulation provides a systematic approach to architecture design, together with sufficient conditions for uniform discretization stability.

Title: VLM as an Editing Pilot: VLM-Steered Velocity Control for Flow-Based Image Editing

  • Keywords: diffusion
  • Abstract: Text-guided image editing requires realizing the requested semantic change while preserving content unrelated to the instruction. Title: Transferring local rib responses to new component geometries in sheet forming with a graph-based Transformer

  • Authors: Yingxue Zhao, Haoran Li, Haosu Zhou, Tobias Pfaff, Nan Li
  • Subjects: cs.LG
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10086
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.10086
  • Copy Paste: [[2611.10086]] Transferring local rib responses to new component geometries in sheet forming with a graph-based Transformer(https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10086)
  • Keywords: transformer
  • Abstract: Sheet metal components often consist of a global geometry, such as the corner radii and forming depth of a corner component, and local features added to it. During forming, both affect material flow. A surrogate learning all their combinations may need large training data. However, the same local feature type is often used on many global geometries. This study therefore partly decouples local features from the global geometry. The local-feature effect is first learned on one global geometry, needing fewer simulations than learning all combinations, then transferred to other global geometries via transfer learning, which can need fewer target simulations than training from scratch. A baseline-conditioned residual surrogate, R-BiLoGo, is proposed for ribs on the side walls of hot-stamped corner components. Its inputs are the baseline response and the geometric perturbation introduced by the ribs. Its outputs are the changes in displacement and thinning from the baseline. R-BiLoGo gave lower errors than a hierarchical graph-based model and a Transolver-based model on the source geometry, and on two of three selected target geometries after transfer. Across the 22 target geometries, transfer learning with 20 target simulations gave errors similar to or lower than training from scratch with 80 target simulations for about half of the targets. With the same 80 target simulations, transfer learning reduced errors for 95% of targets. The effect of the global geometry on transfer was studied with four parameter-free geometric descriptors and controlled variations across 4 corner subclasses. The benefit of transfer decreased with increasing surface distance and high-curvature change between source and target, and for targets shallower than the source. These trends were also seen in other corner types with a different magnitude.

Title: Beyond Prompt Attacks: Least-Action Auditing of Concept Erasure

  • Authors: Yifei Zhu
  • Subjects: cs.CR, cs.CV
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10106
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.10106
  • Copy Paste: [[2611.10106]] Beyond Prompt Attacks: Least-Action Auditing of Concept Erasure(https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10106)
  • Keywords: attack, diffusion
  • Abstract: Concept erasure, or concept unlearning, in text-to-image diffusion models is usually audited with prompt attacks. A prompt attack tests whether one conditioning string still retrieves the erased concept. It does not measure the probability that the frozen generator still assigns to that concept. We introduce least-action auditing of this residual probability, and evaluate it on Stable Diffusion concept-erasure checkpoints. The least-action barrier is the minimum control cost of driving the frozen reverse process into a detector-defined concept event. This cost equals the path-space KL divergence of conditioning the model on the event, and therefore the event's negative log-probability. The estimate $\Delta S_{\mathrm{rob}}$ uses a CLIP$\wedge$DINOv2 consensus terminal, with no retraining and no adversarial prompt. Across $18$ erased concept-mechanism pairs spanning object classes and NSFW nudity, $\Delta S_{\mathrm{rob}}$ predicts deployment leakage counted on $1{,}500$ fresh samples per model by detectors excluded from the audit (pooled Spearman $\rho{=}{-}0.76$, $p{=}5{\times}10^{-4}$). Within every audited concept, the eraser with the higher barrier leaks less ($18/18$). On NSFW nudity the barrier orders the standard erasers exactly: UCE $\Delta S_{\mathrm{rob}}{=}3.9$ (leak $16.4\%$) $<$ ESD $75.5$ ($9.8\%$) $<$ RECE $224.5$ ($1.4\%$), while Safe Latent Diffusion (SLD) stays near zero and leaks $85.5\%$ under explicit prompts. The same trajectories repair deployed checkpoints. An audit-guided closed-form patch reduces UCE nudity leakage from $16.2\%$ to $0/1{,}500$ and RECE church leakage by $3.3\times$, where direction-count-matched random edits fail.

Title: Cryptanalysis of operad-based cryptography

  • Authors: Ambrogio Maria Bernardelli
  • Subjects: cs.CR
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10134
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.10134
  • Copy Paste: [[2611.10134]] Cryptanalysis of operad-based cryptography(https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10134)
  • Keywords: attack
  • Abstract: Commutative operads have been proposed as a platform for public-key cryptography, with protocols for key agreement, key exchange, encryption and authentication, together with a masking technique and families of algebras with secret parameters intended to strengthen them. We show that breaking key agreement, key exchange and encryption reduces to two problems over the centralizer of the public operations: an interpolation problem for key agreement and a division problem for the other two. Neither problem requires recovering a secret, and the set from which secrets are drawn plays no role in these attacks. For the operad platforms in the two original papers (fields, the integers, residue rings, the tropical semiring with finite entries, convexors and the masked tropical algebras), the relevant problems can be solved in polynomial time in the input models specified here, and masking does not affect these attacks. The authentication protocol fails for a more elementary reason: as originally stated it does not bind the prover to any public key, and even with a registered public key the response expected by the verifier can be computed from public data. In the masked variant, verification requires the secret mask; on the concrete string platforms, one observed session with an invertible challenge entry suffices for forgery. We also observe that one of the auxiliary constructions satisfies the algebra axioms only in a degenerate case, although the corresponding protocol remains correct. The attacks are implemented and verified on random instances of representative platforms.

Title: Learning of Tree Automata applied to Neural Language Acceptors

  • Authors: Juan Pedro da Silva, Sergio Yovine
  • Subjects: cs.CL, cs.AI
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10147
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.10147
  • Copy Paste: [[2611.10147]] Learning of Tree Automata applied to Neural Language Acceptors(https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10147)
  • Keywords: transformer
  • Abstract: We extract visibly pushdown grammars (VPGs) from neural language models in a fully black-box setting. Our work builds on the VPL* framework, which learns VPGs from recurrent networks by exploiting access to the target's internal state. First, we replace the white-box equivalence oracle with PAC sampling over trees. The resulting algorithm applies unchanged to transformer language models and, more generally, to any acceptor exposing only binary decisions. Second, we further observe that the original framework only ever queries the target on well-formed sequences that can be accepted by the most permissive VPG (called BParse) with the target's ranked alphabet. That is, VPL* is blind to the target's behavior outside BParse. Thus, we propose to sample trees directly instead of sequences and call the target with all the resulting sequences in order to provide an error estimate of how far the target deviates from being a VPG. Last but not least, we develop a learner that captures a tree automaton describing discovered behaviors of the target outside BParse, which results in a larger learnable space. We evaluate the approach on several cases, including transformers trained with Dyck grammars and synthetic targets designed to be outside BParse.

Title: Learning Across Heterogeneous Feature Spaces with Group Robustness

  • Authors: Xenia Konti, Arjun Tschand, Chuan Hong, Nicoleta J. Economou-Zavlanos, Michael M. Zavlanos
  • Subjects: cs.LG
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10153
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.10153
  • Copy Paste: [[2611.10153]] Learning Across Heterogeneous Feature Spaces with Group Robustness(https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10153)
  • Keywords: robust
  • Abstract: In clinical applications, patients undergo different combinations of tests, and machine learning models must therefore ensure accurate predictions across heterogeneous feature spaces. Current approaches upweight the patients with the highest loss, without accounting for the limited predictive information in their features preventing their loss from going any lower. Moreover, when patients' feature spaces are fully disjoint, current methods reduce to training independent models, preventing knowledge transfer across them. We propose a framework for group robust learning across heterogeneous feature spaces, where groups are patients sharing a feature space. Since a high loss may simply reflect uninformative features, we instead optimize for worst-group excess loss, the gap between a group's loss and an estimate of its achievable optimum. To allow collaboration between groups, we use group-specific encoders mapping into a shared latent space in which learnable Gaussian anchors cluster patients by outcome. On tabular and imaging medical datasets, including fully disjoint feature settings, our framework outperforms state-of-the-art methods when groups differ in their optimal losses or cannot train a good model on their own, reducing worst-group loss by up to 79% and excess loss by up to 95% with up to 18$\times$ fewer parameters.

  • Keywords: attack, robust
  • Abstract: Broken Access Control (BAC) has been the foremost category in the OWASP Top Ten since 2021, primarily due to the challenges in enforcing authorization at the application layer. However, missing or incorrect checks can go undetected by external systems, leaving applications vulnerable. This paper presents BACGUARD, a reverse proxy designed to enforce authorization for unmodified applications. Evaluations on six applications, including those with known vulnerabilities, demonstrate that BACGUARD's hybrid deterministic and language model-based approach blocks all tested attacks with a false-positive rate below 0.7%. Competing solutions like Web Application Firewalls are less effective and more disruptive. The results highlight BACGUARD's primary contribution: the external reconstruction of authorization facts, enabling robust enforcement even when the application itself is flawed.

This paper proposes GSynPEF, a practical framework for empirical evaluation of privacy and utility in settings for which a real-world graph is given as input and output synthetic graph(s) are observed. Title: Projected Neural Additive Models as Universal Approximators

  • Authors: Nhon N. Phan, Qiang Du, John D. Clayton, WaiChing Sun
  • Subjects: cs.LG
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10186
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.10186
  • Copy Paste: [[2611.10186]] Projected Neural Additive Models as Universal Approximators(https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10186)
  • Keywords: interpretability
  • Abstract: Neural additive models (NAMs) offer interpretability but lack expressivity due to their rigid additive structure, whereas multi-layer perceptrons (MLPs) achieve universal approximation at the cost of transparency. We establish the first theoretical foundation for projected neural additive models (PNAMs), which augment NAMs with a learnable linear transformation $\boldsymbol{T}$ to capture cross-variable interactions. While standard NAMs cannot approximate even the simplest interaction term $\chi_1 \chi_2$ because their cross-derivatives are identically zero, we prove that PNAMs are universal approximators and characterize the minimum number of projections required for exact polynomial representation. Furthermore, we derive an approximation rate of $\mathcal{O}(M^{-s / (N - 1)})$ for $\mathcal{C}^s$ functions (i.e., functions with $s$ continuous derivatives), where $M$ denotes the number of projections and $N$ the input dimension; this rate is known to be optimal for ridge function approximation. To recover interpretability from the projected inputs, we introduce structured sparsity regularizers and post-hoc symbolic regression techniques that enable global input ranking, parameter pruning, and the conversion of learned bases into compact analytical expressions. Experiments on knot theory invariants, MNIST, and phase field fracture mechanics demonstrate that PNAMs match or surpass similar architectures in accuracy while discovering salient features and yielding symbolic models with orders of magnitude fewer parameters.

  • Authors: Brendan O Donoghue, Nenad Tomasev, Volodymyr Mnih, Timothy Nguyen, Dalin Guo, Arun Nair, Kenneth Yang, Alexandre Frechette, Jasper Xian, Jean Tarbouriech, Pavel Tyletski, Ingrid von Glehn, Johannes Bausch, Demis Hassabis, Raia Hadsell

  • Keywords: security, diffusion, transformer, generative
  • Abstract: We demonstrate that transformer architectures trained with discrete denoising diffusion and decoded via guided test-time beam search can learn to recover the prime factors of semiprime integers directly from data. Our approach establishes a new frontier for learning-based semiprime factorization, successfully factoring 68-bit integers-a substantial advance over the prior 56-bit state of the art. While computationally less efficient than classical factoring algorithms, our model requires no domain-specific number-theoretic priors and learns purely from training examples. In practice, the diffusion model acts as a generative prior, identifying promising factor candidates where exact verification or lattice-based post-processing can complete the factorization. Furthermore, we derive empirical scaling laws relating approximation quality to training data volume, model size, and test-time computation. In contrast to language, problem size is the key driver of scaling behavior. Achieving a fixed accuracy on a held-out test set requires compute that scales exponentially with bit length. Consequently, our findings pose no threat to security, as the projected compute cost required to factorize cryptographically relevant integers remains prohibitive.

Distributed SSB optimization is still at an early stage, with open questions about convergence guarantees and communication efficiency. To fill this gap, we propose the Distributed Momentum-based Spectral Estimator (DisMUSE), an efficient distributed SSB optimization framework for both data-center and federated settings. Using Muon-style exact orthogonalized momentum as the spectral update, DisMUSE integrates a recursive variance-reduced momentum estimator and achieves an $O((KT)^{-1/3})$ convergence rate, where $K$ and $T$ denote the number of nodes and local iterations per node, respectively. This rate matches the best-known rates for variance-reduced nonconvex distributed optimization. Building on this variance-reduced framework, we study two extensions. First, our DisMUSE method with finite Newton-Schulz (NS) iterations preserves the same convergence order under a uniform normalized support-spectrum condition, up to a multiplicative factor. Second, we develop a communication-efficient variant based on low-rank (LR) momentum transmission and quantify the error it introduces to the stationarity bound, characterizing the trade-off between accuracy and communication cost.

Title: RAD-TFM: Robust and Domain-Adapted Tabular Foundation Models

  • Authors: Matthew Peroni, Franck Le, Vadim Sheinin
  • Subjects: cs.LG, cs.AI
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10230
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.10230
  • Copy Paste: [[2611.10230]] RAD-TFM: Robust and Domain-Adapted Tabular Foundation Models(https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10230)
  • Keywords: robust
  • Abstract: The development of tabular foundation models (TFMs) has accelerated in recent years, outperforming traditional ML methods on many tabular benchmarks. A key finding is that TFMs can be pretrained entirely on synthetic datasets, opening opportunities to design data generators that encourage desirable model properties. Prior work has mainly focused on crafting high-quality priors over generators to improve overall pretraining performance. Our key insight is that parameterizing the generator distribution enables an adversarial, distributional robustness perspective: during training, we can adapt the generator to emphasize datasets that are particularly challenging for the model, which we formalize by introducing an optimality gap measure. Applied to the TabPFN V2 classifier, RAD-TFM improves performance across 6 diverse tabular benchmarks, with up to a 10.8 percentage-point improvement in mean normalized AUC over the strongest baseline, using fewer than 100k additional synthetic training datasets, less than 0.1% of the original pretraining data. Applying the same pipeline to TabICL V2 also improves performance across three major benchmarks, demonstrating the generality of the framework. These results highlight a promising new direction for targeted adversarial continued training of TFMs using synthetic data alone.

  • Abstract: Probabilistic neural surrogates for PDEs enable ensemble forecasting, but often exhibit miscalibration when used for uncertainty quantification, especially in compute-constrained, few-step inference regimes. We propose TORNADO, a latent probabilistic forecasting framework that formulates next-step prediction as stochastic transport between VAE encoder posteriors.   • Authors: Adam Hazimeh, Amel Abdelraheem, Ke Wang, Mariam Salman, Ljiljana Dolamic, Gérôme Bovet, Pascal Frossard

  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10258
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.10258
  • Copy Paste: [[2611.10258]] Beyond the Prompt: Leveraging Pre-Decoding States for Jailbreak Detection in dLLMs(https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10258)
  • Keywords: attack, diffusion, large language model
  • Abstract: Diffusion large language models (dLLMs) have recently emerged as a strong alternative to autoregressive models, generating responses by iteratively refining masked tokens in parallel rather than decoding sequentially. Title: STAIRS: Staging the Inverse Problem via Admissible-Support Scheduling for Unrolled MRI Reconstruction

  • Authors: Xiangjian Hou, Chang Ni, Chao Qin, Zhiwei Tan, Xin Wang, Xinrui Ye, Rong-Rong Chen, Chun Yuan, Xiaodong Ma
  • Subjects: cs.CV
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10260
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.10260

  • Keywords: transformer
  • Abstract: Accelerated magnetic resonance imaging (MRI) forms images from incomplete Fourier measurements, shifting acquisition burden to computational reconstruction. Deep unrolled networks interleave learned regularization and data consistency, yet typically estimate the full unmeasured spectrum at every cascade. We propose STAIRS (Staging the Inverse Problem via Admissible-Support Scheduling), which projects intermediate reconstructions onto nested Fourier supports that expand to the full spectrum and conditions each update on its support transition. Conditional increments of a regularized log-determinant score of training covariance define a normalized spectral coordinate, accounting for dependencies between newly and previously admitted frequencies. Dyadic targets allocate larger coordinate increments to earlier cascades and are mapped to fixed supports before training, enabling coarse-to-fine reconstruction with unchanged sampling masks and data consistency. Stage-wise analysis shows repeated error correction by projection and lower intermediate reconstruction errors than the backbones alone throughout support expansion. Ablations favor dyadic allocation over uniform expansion for square and line-band supports and over manual front-loading for square windows. Support projection alone improves reconstruction, and transition conditioning strengthens the gain. Cross-dataset evaluation shows improvements for both backbones in both directions. Support allocation thus complements regularizer design in unrolled MRI reconstruction.

  • Abstract: Languages use different morphosyntactic strategies to express the same meaning.   • Authors: Ada Canoğlu, Sadiq Qara
  • Subjects: cs.CL
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10319
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.10319

  • Keywords: large language model
  • Abstract: Logical reasoning over natural language requires both semantic interpretation and deductive search. Large language models (LLMs) can propose plausible intermediate derivations, but they often fail to compose them into a complete proof and overpredict uncertainty. Neurosymbolic methods improve search by translating an entire problem into first-order logic and handing it to a prover, but a single bad clause can make the whole translation nonexecutable. We present Hybrid Reasoning, a three-stage pipeline that decouples proposing a proof, validating its steps, and deciding a label. An LLM proposes a structured proof tree with a raw verdict; a theorem prover validates each derivation locally, keeping only the subset it can prove; and an uncertainty-gated repair attempts to re-derive the label from the premises plus the validated derivations, when the raw verdict is UNCERTAIN. The repair stage operates at 78-98% precision, producing 326 correct relabellings against 19 regressions.

Title: PoseBench: Probing Pose Awareness in Visual Foundation Models

  • Authors: Most Nilufa Yeasmin, Draix Wyatt, Ruyi Lian
  • Subjects: cs.CV, cs.AI
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10347

  • Keywords: robust
  • Abstract: Pose awareness characterizes the extent to which a learned feature space aligns with the underlying object-pose manifold, such that observations with similar poses exhibit greater feature similarity than those with increasingly different poses. While such structure is desirable for pose-sensitive 3D vision tasks, visual foundation models pre-trained on individual 2D images typically lack explicit 3D or pose supervision. This raises a fundamental question: Do 2D visual foundation models exhibit pose awareness after large-scale pretraining? To answer this question, we introduce PoseBench, a systematic benchmark to probe pose awareness in pretrained visual features. Our evaluation follows a progressive design: held-out rendered views probe intrinsic pose-aware structure under controlled conditions, while real-image queries evaluate how robustly this structure remains accessible under real-world variations. We further introduce pose-graph-based refinement as a complementary probe of compatibility between feature neighborhoods and the underlying pose manifold. Across 31 publicly available checkpoints from 10 pretraining families, PoseBench reveals that pose awareness is an implicit but non-uniform property of 2D visual foundation models, and consistent gains from pose-graph refinement suggest that feature spaces retain structure compatible with the underlying pose manifold. PoseBench therefore provides a systematic basis for understanding and selecting pretrained features for pose-sensitive 3D vision tasks.

Title: FEROM: Frontier Endogenous Reveal-Order Marginal Policy Optimization for Masked Diffusion LMs

  • Authors: Zian Su, Ziyang Huang, Kaiyuan Zhang, Xiangyu Zhang
  • Subjects: cs.CL, cs.AI
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10369
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.10369

  • Keywords: diffusion
  • Abstract: Masked diffusion language models generate text by iteratively unmasking positions, with practical samplers often selecting reveal positions from the model's own logits. The reveal order is therefore an endogenous latent variable of the rollout process with an enormous discrete latent space. Policy optimization objectives relying on pre-defined schedules or sampled trajectories introduce either bias or high variance. In this paper, we propose FEROM, Frontier Endogenous Reveal-Order Marginal Policy Optimization, which approximates policy gradients for the rollout-induced marginal response policy of masked diffusion LMs. FEROM derives a Rao-Blackwellized policy-gradient identity over latent reveal orders and expresses the resulting estimator as posterior edge occupancy on a reveal-state DAG. To make marginalization practical, we introduce Frontier Reveal Marginalization, a budgeted estimator that approximates this marginal edge structure by combining CDF-based computation of local probabilities of the endogenous reveal process with frontier expansion of high-mass partial states. Integrated into a GRPO-style objective, FEROM replaces single-path log-scores with a detached token-score surrogate over posterior-relevant reveal edges. Experiments on math and coding tasks show comparable or improved results over existing methods under matched scoring-state budgets. An offline proxy study further shows improved marginal-gradient approximation with increased scoring budgets.

  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10393
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.10393

  • Keywords: robust
  • Abstract: Estimating the principal eigenpair of a positive operator from stochastic evaluations gives rise to a noisy form of normalized power iteration: each step applies a sampled operator estimate and then renormalizes. We establish finite-time stochastic-approximation guarantees for this procedure, working in Hilbert's projective metric, which is well-suited to the multiplicative geometry of Perron--Frobenius eigenproblems. Our main application is risk-sensitive average-cost reinforcement learning (RL) with exponential utility. In this setting the Bellman equations are multiplicative, and value functions are characterized by nonlinear eigenvalue problems rather than additive fixed points. We cast both policy evaluation and control in this framework, obtaining risk-sensitive TD- and Q-learning algorithms that learn from a single Markovian trajectory. Under explicit positivity, mixing, and linear function approximation assumptions, we prove finite-time bounds on the recovered eigenvector and eigenvalue with $\tilde{O}(\epsilon^{-2})$ sample complexity up to problem-dependent constants. The results cover both tabular and linear function approximation regimes, and through the duality between exponential utility and KL-robust control yield finite-time guarantees for KL-robust average-cost RL as well.

Title: The Limits of Information-Flow Control Enforcement for Agentic Systems

  • Keywords: security
  • Abstract: Information-flow control (IFC) is a promising approach to enforce policy in agentic AI systems. However, IFC suffers from label creep, a phenomenon where monotonically increasing security labels propagate through a system and progressively limit its utility. Label creep in agentic AI systems can be mitigated using two major strategies: (1) improved system design and (2) label declassification. In this paper, we characterize how far system design alone can mitigate label creep in agentic systems. Specifically, we measure how system topology, scale, and agent composition affect utility loss and security enforcement cost proxies for conservative IFC enforcement in a stateful agentic system simulator. These findings identify design choices to reduce IFC utility loss, but they also demonstrate that system design alone cannot fully prevent utility loss due to monotonic label propagation. This motivates the development of label declassification mechanisms that aid these design choices and diminish IFC utility cost.

Title: Mental Imagery That Matters: Curing Latent Collapse in Multimodal Reasoning

  • Authors: Mi Luo, Rohan Dugad, Zihui Xue, Alex Dimakis, Kristen Grauman
  • Subjects: cs.CV, cs.AI
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10423
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.10423
  • Copy Paste: [[2611.10423]] Mental Imagery That Matters: Curing Latent Collapse in Multimodal Reasoning(https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10423)
  • Keywords: large language model
  • Abstract: Mental simulation, the ability to reason by constructing and updating internal visual states, is central to flexible human cognition, but remains elusive in current multimodal large language models. Latent multimodal reasoning offers a promising route toward this capability by interleaving text with continuous visual latent blocks, allowing models to maintain internal visual workspaces without decoding them into pixels. In this paper, we ask whether these latent visual states actually matter. Through a direct perturbation analysis across representative latent-reasoning MLLMs, we find that they often do not: dropping, replacing, or shuffling generated visual latents has only minor effects on the model's own final-answer probability, while equivalent perturbations to the linguistic reasoning trace are substantially more damaging. We call this failure mode Mental Simulation Collapse (MSC): the model appears to perform interleaved visual reasoning, but routes most answer-relevant computation through text. We further diagnose that MSC follows naturally from existing training objectives: visual alignment can make generated latents resemble helper-image embeddings, but it does not require the decoder to rely on them; subsequent text-only supervision further leaves the semantic role of the latent pathway unidentifiable. To address this, we introduce AnchorSim (Anchored Mental Simulation), a contrastive distillation framework that trains models to make their generated visual latents consequential. Across multiple visual reasoning benchmarks, AnchorSim improves performance, while diagnostic re-evaluation shows that visual perturbations now meaningfully affect predictions. Our results expose a fundamental failure mode of latent multimodal reasoning and demonstrate that mental simulation must be anchored to model behavior in order to matter.

  • Abstract: Video diffusion transformers generate high-quality videos, but attention over long spatiotemporal sequences makes inference computationally expensive. We present ESTRA (Extreme Sparsity Through Refined Attention), a training-free acceleration method that preserves generation quality even at extreme sparsity. Methods: In this retrospective study across four imaging modalities, chest radiography, fundus photography, and CT models were trained only on synthetic images and externally validated on real-world data against real-data-trained models. Carotid ultrasound models were trained on a private Human Phenotype Project cohort (aged 40-75 years) with and without synthetic augmentation. Clinicians rated image plausibility. Evaluation used AUROC, AUPR, calibration, and decision curve analysis. Findings: Synthetic-only models achieved AUROCs of 0.877 (95% CI 0.843-0.910) for pneumonia (n=14863), 0.963 (0.948-0.975) for retinal detachment (n=1149), and 0.711 (0.600-0.823) for renal calculus (n=4122), comparable to real-data-trained models, but their calibration and net benefit were not consistently more favorable. With mixed synthetic-real training, a carotid ultrasound model produced an ordinal vascular age index that stratified 633 external subjects into subgroups differing in BMI and glucose (both p<0.01), linking vascular aging to cardiometabolic health. To stress-test shortcut learning, editing a moderate tessellated pattern into fundus photographs reduced the true-class probability for central serous chorioretinopathy from 0.996 to 0.822. Clinician-guided editing raised low-quality CT plausibility from 3.10/5 to 4.30/5. Interpretation: Synthetic data can complement real data in model training, subgroup representation, and model testing via controlled editing, but its clinical value should be assessed with calibration, decision curve analysis, and clinician review, not discrimination alone.

Title: Decoupling Direction and Magnitude: Language-Steered Flow Matching for Super-Resolution in the Dark

  • Authors: Jiaxin Gao, Ziyu Yue, Yaohua Liu, Danchen Cui, Zhihui Zhao, Zhixun Su
  • Subjects: cs.CV
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10485
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.10485
  • Copy Paste: [[2611.10485]] Decoupling Direction and Magnitude: Language-Steered Flow Matching for Super-Resolution in the Dark(https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10485)
  • Keywords: diffusion, generative
  • Abstract: Image super-resolution in the dark is fundamentally challenged by extreme spatial heterogeneity: severely underexposed and noisy regions demand aggressive generative enhancement to reconstruct missing details, while relatively well-exposed areas require conservative updates to prevent hallucinated textures. Standard diffusion and flow-matching models, however, rely on globally uniform integration trajectories, making them sub-optimal for such spatially varying degradations. In this paper, we propose DeLang-SR (Degradation-Language steered Super-Resolution), a one-step flow-matching framework that explicitly decouples the restoration direction and magnitude. Rather than treating low-light corruption as arbitrary latent features, DeLang-SR translates measurable physical statistics into structured degradation language, harnessing the semantic prior of pretrained text-to-image models. The degradation-language intent, complemented by image-specific visual tokens, steers the velocity field (direction) toward restoration-relevant manifolds. Simultaneously, a continuous spatial update-strength field acts as a locally varying Euler step-size field (magnitude), applying stronger integration steps in heavily degraded shadows while enforcing conservative updates in reliable regions. Experiments on paired and real dark images, together with ablations and diagnostic analyses, show that DeLang-SR provides an effective and interpretable one-step route to super-resolution in the dark. Code and pretrained models are available at this https URL.

  • Abstract: Diffusion language models (DLMs) generate text through iterative denoising, where decisions about which tokens to commit or remask strongly affect output quality. We formulate DLM test-time scaling as adaptive allocation of both inference compute and decoding strategies. Our two-stage approach uses a fast base sampler to solve easy questions, then applies frequency-aware remasking to the remaining hard questions. On questions unsolved by the base certainty-aware strategy after 100 samples, frequency-aware remasking recovers solutions for 3.75% to 18.75% of examples.

Title: Disentangling Channel Semantics in Vision Transformers via Token Decorrelation and Composition-Aware Modulation

  • Abstract: Multi-channel images encode heterogeneous channel semantics aligned over a shared spatial structure, which requires models to jointly capture globally shared structure and channel-specific variations. However, we find that these context tokens collapse onto a few dominant channels under high inter-channel redundancy, producing redundant representations and a biased global summary that fails to reliably support composition-aware channel interpretation. Channel Composition-Aware Modulation (CAM) uses the FTL-refined [CLS] as a semantic anchor to generate sample-wise modulation signals, which enable composition-aware interpretation of channels within a unified backbone rather than relying on static channel embeddings. Experiments on JUMP-CP, CHAMMI, and So2Sat show that MuCa-ViT outperforms prior multi-channel ViTs across all three benchmarks, and the magnitude of improvement aligns with the inter-channel redundancy of each dataset ($\rho \in [0.19, 0.77]$), validating the diagnostic analysis that motivates our design.   • Authors: Jerjes Aguirre-Chavez, Shant Malkasian, Roshun Sankaran, Brendan Crabb, Albert Hsiao
  • Subjects: cs.CL

  • Keywords: extraction, large language model
  • Abstract: Large language models (LLMs) can structure clinical datasets, but extraction error must be distinguished from discordance in the underlying data. Title: SRA: Spatial Reasoning Adapter via Evolving Social Interaction Graphs for Trajectory Prediction

  • Authors: Jaewoo Jeong, Seonkyu Song, Hyeonwoo Park, Jegyeong Cho, Yujin Bae, Giwon Lee, Daehee Park, Kuk-Jin Yoon
  • Subjects: cs.CV

  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.10506

  • Keywords: diffusion, generative
  • Abstract: Predicting where multiple agents will move next is fundamental to autonomous driving, robotics, and any system that must share space with people safely. We address this gap with SRA (Spatial Reasoning Adapter), a modular graph denoiser that is integrated into existing diffusion and flow-matching predictors without redesigning their generative cores. At every reverse step, SRA reads the host's current future estimate, builds a sparse Euclidean interaction graph on the predicted trajectories, and returns a residual correction to the host's decoding path. Our adapter consists of three core components: a sparse neighborhood retrieved with a learned query-key score and a geometric bias, a future-conditioned relational feature recomputed on the evolving prediction, and uncertainty-weighted message passing where confident agents act as anchors for uncertain ones. SRA improves ADE for every host on every dataset, with average gains of 5.8% in ADE and 7.0% in FDE. A cumulative ablation shows that the three components act as one mechanism: only together do they improve every host on every sports dataset, indicating that spatial reasoning over the model's own evolving future, weighted by how far that future can be trusted, is broadly useful across denoising architectures. Code is available at this https URL.

  • Abstract: Diffusion Transformers (DiTs) have established themselves as the preeminent paradigm for high-fidelity generative modeling across various modalities, yet their practical deployment is hindered by the severe computational overhead of iterative sampling. In this work, we reveal that DiT feature evolution exhibits anomalous diffusion characteristics and a certain degree of spatiotemporal heterogeneity, rendering static, integer-order forecasting operators inadequate and prone to error accumulation.
