Leveraging the empirical observation that these complex dynamics maintain cross-sample stability, we further construct a Look-Up Table with multiple dimensions through offline profiling. This enables negligible LUT-retrieval overhead, spatiotemporally adaptive parameter retrieval during online inference. Extensive evaluations across diverse architectures and modalities demonstrate that FADE achieves state-of-the-art acceleration, preserving exceptional generation fidelity under aggressive skip intervals with negligible latency overhead. Title: When Experience Becomes a Backdoor: Poisoning Self-Evolving LLM Agents via Failure-Aware Task Optimization

  • Authors: Minhua Lin, Zongyu Wu, Hanqing Lu, Xianfeng Tang, Xiang Zhang, Qi He, Suhang Wang
  • Subjects: cs.CR, cs.AI
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10542
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.10542
  • Copy Paste: [[2611.10542]] When Experience Becomes a Backdoor: Poisoning Self-Evolving LLM Agents via Failure-Aware Task Optimization(https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10542)
  • Keywords: attack, large language model
  • Abstract: Large language model (LLM) agents increasingly improve themselves through self-evolution, distilling execution experience into persistent, reusable skills. However, this persistence creates a new attack surface: malicious rules implanted into skills can compromise subsequent agent behavior. Existing skill backdoor attacks typically assume direct manipulation of the victim's skills, which is impractical when skills are constructed internally by a trusted evolver. In this paper, we study a novel problem of task-level backdoor attacks, where the attacker can only inject poisoned evolution tasks, inducing the agent to implant backdoors into its own skills. Since the attacker controls neither skill induction nor downstream execution, a natural approach is to iteratively optimize poisoned tasks based on attack success. However, our preliminary analysis reveals two key challenges: (i) failures at different stages of skill evolution and downstream execution can produce indistinguishable outcomes, providing little guidance for targeted poisoned-task updates; and (ii) unsuccessful updates may undermine earlier progress, while simply discarding them may lead to repeated ineffective updates. To address these challenges, we propose BadEvolve, a failure-aware task-poisoning framework against self-evolving LLM agents. BadEvolve diagnoses attack failures across four stages, i.e., Induction, Activation, Following, and Effect, and directs updates toward the failing stage. It further employs rollback to preserve earlier progress and failure memory to avoid repeating ineffective updates. Extensive experiments across diverse self-evolution frameworks, benchmarks, and backbone LLMs demonstrate the effectiveness of BadEvolve. Our code is available at this https URL.

Title: Dual-Granularity Learning for Regression with Continuous Noisy Labels

  • Authors: Hui Guo, Boyu Wang, Grace Y. Yi
  • Subjects: cs.LG
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10550
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.10550
  • Copy Paste: [[2611.10550]] Dual-Granularity Learning for Regression with Continuous Noisy Labels(https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10550)
  • Keywords: robust
  • Abstract: Learning with noisy labels has been extensively studied for classification, yet its regression counterpart remains relatively underexplored. Unlike classification, regression involves continuous-valued targets where label noise exhibits heterogeneous magnitudes across instances, which poses conflicting requirements on the model: correcting small-to-moderate deviations requires sensitivity to label values for instance-level refinement, whereas large deviations caused by corrupted or outlying annotations render exact label values unreliable, necessitating reduced reliance on exact labels and instead leveraging ordinal structure among samples. These requirements call for methods that capture both global structure and sample-specific deviations in a unified manner. To this end, we propose GRAIN, a dual-granularity framework for robust noisy regression. At the fine-grained level, we derive an explicit residual learning mechanism from a robust loss formulation, introducing instance-specific auxiliary variables to decouple label noise from predictive signals. At the coarse-grained level, we introduce a binning-based contrastive regularization that enforces an order-preserving feature structure aligned with label proximity, addressing noise beyond the capacity of local residual refinement. The two levels are coupled via a histogram-based bridging mechanism that enables bidirectional information flow: coarse-grained structural guidance establishes a well-ordered feature space that anchors residual learning, while fine-grained residual refinement corrects instance-level deviations that structural regularization alone cannot resolve. Experimental results show that GRAIN consistently improves robustness and generalization under diverse noise conditions.

Title: Large Language Models without QK-Normalization

  • Authors: Yongding Tao, Yitong Qiao, Tianqi Shao, Tian Wang, Zhixuan Chu, Xiaoming Wu, Yihong Dong, Ge Li
  • Subjects: cs.CL, cs.AI, cs.LG
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10560
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.10560
  • Copy Paste: [[2611.10560]] Large Language Models without QK-Normalization(https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10560)
  • Keywords: large language model
  • Abstract: Query-Key Normalization (QK-Norm) is widely adopted in modern large language models to control the growth of attention logits and improve training stability. However, query and key norms are not merely sources of instability. The query norm controls the sharpness of the attention distribution, while the key norm determines how strongly a token attracts different queries. These norms therefore carry token-specific information about attention selectivity and token importance. By normalizing both, QK-Norm suppresses this information. This raises a natural question: can we control query-key representations without discarding their sensitivity to magnitude? To address this question, we propose QK-MAP, a Magnitude-Aware Projection for constructing queries and keys. Rather than removing magnitude through normalization, QK-MAP transforms the input representation using multiple learnable frequencies, so that changes in magnitude are encoded as bounded phase patterns. This allows magnitude to be represented through phase and direction rather than vector length alone. Its periodic features have a fixed norm while remaining sensitive to magnitude changes, separating magnitude encoding from norm growth. Multiple frequencies further help distinguish magnitudes and reduce periodic ambiguity. Experiments across model scales from 190M to 7B parameters show that QK-MAP achieves lower training and validation loss than both Standard attention and QK-Norm. Further analyses show that QK-MAP preserves sensitivity to input scale, assigns less attention to the first token, and produces stronger attention contrast for relevant evidence in synthetic long-context tests. These results demonstrate that query-key scale can be effectively controlled without sacrificing token-specific magnitude information.

Title: Design and Evaluation of Secure Distributed Chaotic Image Encryption on ESP32-S3

  • Authors: Mohammed Almozaini, Fazal Noor, Sohail Alhazimi
  • Subjects: cs.CR
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10595
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.10595
  • Copy Paste: [[2611.10595]] Design and Evaluation of Secure Distributed Chaotic Image Encryption on ESP32-S3(https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10595)
  • Keywords: secure, security
  • Abstract: The limited computational resources of Internet of Things (IoT) devices present challenges for secure image processing. This paper presents a distributed chaotic image encryption system using independent Wi-Fi-connected ESP32-S3 microcontrollers. A master-worker architecture distributes image blocks through master-coordinated chaotic sequence management, maintaining one logically continuous chaotic sequence by assigning block-specific starting states independently of worker assignments. The implemented system uses reversible chaotic substitution (SUBSTITUTE), while Elliptic Curve Digital Signature Algorithm (ECDSA)-signed discovery messages and Transport Layer Security (TLS) 1.3 with mutual authentication secure device communication. Experimental evaluation using one, two, and four workers demonstrates a reduction in execution time from 43.215 to 15.878 seconds, achieving a speed-up of 2.72 times. The encrypted image achieves a Shannon entropy of 7.999940 bits/byte and near-zero adjacent-pixel correlation. Exact image recovery is verified through matching Secure Hash Algorithm 256-bit (SHA-256) hashes and a Structural Similarity Index (SSIM) value of 1.0. Additional experiments demonstrate successful encryption and decryption using different worker counts and block sizes, automatic task reassignment following worker failure, and rejection of a mismatched private key during TLS authentication. These findings demonstrate the feasibility of secure, flexible, and distributed chaotic image processing across independent resource-constrained IoT devices.

  • Keywords: large language model
  • Abstract: Long video understanding requires models to identify and reason over sparse, temporally distributed evidence within limited frame budgets. Existing keyframe selection methods typically retrieve query-relevant frames from a densely sampled candidate set before inference. However, global image-text matching incurs unnecessary computation when a small set of uniformly sampled frames already provides sufficient evidence. Query relevance alone may also overlook evidence that distinguishes competing answers, while one-shot selection offers no opportunity to correct earlier selection errors.   • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10620

  • Keywords: large language model
  • Abstract: Activation probes look like near-perfect monitors for secret collusion among large language model (LLM) agents. We turn the controls into a protocol for testing any collusion monitor, and adapt scan statistics into an online monitor for groups of any size.

  • Authors: Aastha A K Verma, Anwoy Chatterjee, Sparsh Jain, Tanmoy Chakraborty
  • Subjects: cs.CL, cs.LG
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10646
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.10646

  • Keywords: transformer
  • Abstract: Transformer-based autoregressive multilingual language models (LMs) are often assumed to store factual knowledge in an interlingua: a language-agnostic latent space. If this assumption holds, a parameter update applied in a source language during knowledge editing should inherently propagate to its semantic equivalents in target languages. However, empirical benchmarks show that cross-lingual propagation of edits often degrades substantially, a degradation that prior work treats largely as an algorithmic symptom rather than a structural bottleneck. In this work, we investigate whether representational anisotropy creates a structural bottleneck for source-only associative-memory editing, and perform a geometric dissection of the cross-lingual propagation gap. Our empirical findings show that cross-lingual propagation is primarily bottlenecked by a large orthogonal residual component, indicating that source-language edits often fail to span the target-language update directions required for successful transfer. Furthermore, we show that post-hoc correction using only source-language residual statistics encounters a large residual-prediction barrier. Finally, we perform a structural realignment via a targeted contrastive alignment objective and find that it substantially improves key-space propagation and partially improves cross-lingual edit transfer, while leaving value-space mismatch as a remaining bottleneck.

Title: MACaQ: Fast, Model Agnostic Calibrated Quantization for Low-Precision Weights and Activations

  • Authors: Lorenz K. Muller, Philippe Bich, Chiara Boretti, Hyun-Min Chang, Jiawei Zhuang, Lukas Cavigelli
  • Subjects: cs.LG
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10649
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.10649

  • Keywords: diffusion
  • Abstract: Weight-activation quantization can reduce the memory and compute cost of neural-network inference, but recent methods often require expensive optimization and architecture-specific propagation of basis transforms across layers. We introduce MACaQ, an MXFP4 W4A4 post-training quantization method that treats every linear layer independently. MACaQ applies a per-channel scale and a block-Hadamard transform online to each layer's activations, while absorbing the corresponding inverse transforms into its weights. Retaining these transforms online avoids cross-layer dependencies and enables a simple streaming implementation of the quantization process, in which only one layer must reside in memory at a time. To improve over round-to-nearest quantization, we introduce Flip-Refinement, a Hessian-aware coordinate-descent procedure that greedily moves individual FP4 codes to adjacent representable values. MACaQ reaches lower perplexity than DuQuant++ and MR-GPTQ, and already matches their best perplexity on Llama-3-8B in 11 times less quantization time. We further apply MACaQ without architecture-specific modifications to an image diffusion model, illustrating the generality of its layer-local design and show quantization of a 122B parameter model in 64 minutes, using only 32.5GB GPU RAM.

Title: Heterogeneous Asymmetric Contrastive Distillation

  • Authors: Qi Wang, Jinjia Zhou
  • Subjects: cs.CV
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10672
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.10672
  • Copy Paste: [[2611.10672]] Heterogeneous Asymmetric Contrastive Distillation(https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10672)
  • Keywords: transformer
  • Abstract: Knowledge distillation (KD) between architectural families, e.g., from a Transformer teacher to a CNN student, is difficult because the two networks learn different representations. Logits share a label space, but heterogeneous teachers differ in logit offset, scale, and sharpness, and their non-target classes are less semantically organized. Conventional logit distillation matches these values sample by sample, asking the student to imitate how another architecture expresses its predictions. We argue that a heterogeneous teacher should pass on the relational structure of its predictions, i.e., how it arranges samples relative to one another, and propose Heterogeneous Asymmetric Contrastive Distillation (HACD) to transfer this structure. HACD maps teacher and student logits to centered, temperature-softened probability directions on a shared unit hypersphere, which removes architecture-dependent offsets and lets the temperature control how sharply peaked outputs enter the representation while keeping informative non-target classes. Each student output must then identify its own teacher target within the batch. Because every teacher target also acts as a negative for the others, HACD computes the targets on a moderately augmented view and contrasts both a moderate and a strong student view against them. HACD adds no learnable parameters and needs one teacher pass per sample. It achieves the best or second-best accuracy on 11 of 12 heterogeneous pairs on CIFAR-100, without ground-truth labels, and on 14 of 15 pairs on ImageNet-1K, improving over the reported vanilla KD results by 8.82 and 1.33 percentage points on average. Checkpoints are available at this https URL the code will be released upon publication.

Title: JoVox: Joint Hierarchical Flow Matching for Lightweight Voxel Generation

  • Authors: Xiangjun Tang, Jianqi Chen, Peter Wonka
  • Subjects: cs.CV, cs.GT
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10690
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.10690

  • Abstract: Generating high-fidelity voxels while keeping their lightweight nature remains an open problem in computer vision. Current generators are either high-fidelity but computationally heavy, or lightweight but of lower fidelity. Hierarchical methods keep the lightweight nature of voxels. However, their coarse levels, which decide where all finer cells can appear, are generated with the least information about the shape and are never revisited, so they are often plausible but wrong. Our key insight is to let finer cells take part in deciding coarse cells. To this end, we propose JoVox, a discrete flow matching framework that learns the joint distribution of all levels of the voxel hierarchy, instead of a separate distribution for each level, so that coarse cells can be predicted with finer cells already generated nearby. Within this framework, we further introduce a two-level prediction, which outputs the cells two levels finer at once instead of one level finer, halving the number of levels that the network processes and yielding voxels of higher fidelity. We also introduce an unmasking strategy that revisits early decisions once finer cells are generated and corrects inconsistencies in masked diffusion models. Our framework outperforms the baselines on both unconditional and image-conditioned voxel generation. Compared with the current state-of-the-art TRELLIS.2, we achieve 8x higher training throughput, 1/15 of the FLOPs in a forward pass, train on 4 vs. 32 GPUs, and obtain higher quality with an 11% lower Chamfer distance.

Title: A Geometric Theory for Nonequilibrium Transformer Dynamics

  • Authors: Aythami Bethencourt de León, Darryl D. Holm
  • Subjects: cs.LG, math-ph, math.PR, nlin.AO, stat.ML
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10691

  • Copy Paste: [[2611.10691]] A Geometric Theory for Nonequilibrium Transformer Dynamics(https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10691)
  • Keywords: transformer
  • Abstract: We develop a variational and geometric theory based on statistical physics for the joint evolution of Transformer attention and token representations at fixed model parameters. To this end, we introduce a free energy for the attention and model the evolution of the attention weights by its Fisher--Rao gradient flow. At fixed token representations, we prove that the softmax matrix is the unique free-energy minimiser and that every positive attention trajectory converges to it. For prescribed time-dependent representations, we show that the attention dynamics becomes linear after a change to logit variables. This result yields tracking bounds relative to the instantaneous softmax and sufficient conditions for attention to converge to a fixed matrix. Hamiltonian dynamics for the representations and their momenta are then coupled to the attention relaxation driven by the same energy. This coupling results in an energy-dissipation law. We also develop two alternative Hamiltonians incorporating the attention value map. We prove global well-posedness of the attention flow and all three coupled systems.

Title: AbductionBench: Benchmarking Abductive Reasoning in Large Language Models

  • Authors: Moein Salimi, Amir Qeysarbeigi, Babak Hosseini Mohtasham, Faraz Doagoye Tehrani, Mahdi Naieni, Hanieh Sartipi, Ali Nouri, Amin Aghakasiri, Reyhane Khayatzade Mahani, Mahdi Jafari Siavoshani, Mohammad Hossein Rohban
  • Subjects: cs.LG
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10705
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.10705
  • Copy Paste: [[2611.10705]] AbductionBench: Benchmarking Abductive Reasoning in Large Language Models(https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10705)
  • Keywords: large language model
  • Abstract: Abductive reasoning is the inference of plausible explanations for observed evidence. Evaluating this capability requires more than a single task: a system may need to generate an explanation, discriminate among competing hypotheses, revise its hypotheses as evidence arrives, or decide what evidence to seek next. Yet these capabilities are typically evaluated through isolated benchmarks with different task formulations and scoring procedures, so claims that a model is ``good at abduction'' may reflect only a narrow aspect of this capability. We introduce AbductionBench, a unified framework whose taxonomy characterizes abductive tasks. We instantiate this framework on 27 datasets spanning ten domains and evaluate eight LLMs from four model families evaluated on single-choice selection tasks. Beyond harmonizing heterogeneous tasks, AbductionBench separates final-answer evaluation from analysis of the observable reasoning traces and interaction histories produced alongside those answers. We analyze written traces as observable model outputs rather than faithful representations of internal computation, characterizing evidence use, hypothesis comparison, uncertainty, contradiction handling, and answer commitment. Jev matches or exceeds small LLMs when selecting among explanations over compact evidence but falls substantially behind on longer cases, suggesting that candidate discrimination and extended evidence integration place different demands on a system. In interactive tasks, the proportion of actions judged relevant declines at later steps, and a relevant first question is associated with a correct final answer; in the passive task, models rarely revise their initial hypothesis as new evidence arrives. Together, these findings highlight the value of evaluating abductive reasoning through both final answers and observable evidence use, hypothesis revision, and information gathering.

  • Abstract: With a solid theoretical foundation and pretraining on large-scale datasets, diffusion-based methods have achieved controllable generation leveraging text and image conditions, and generating excellent stylized images is possible using diffusion models. Title: CyberBinGym: Can AI Agents Turn Binary Patches into Real Exploits?

  • Authors: Lambang Akbar Wijayadi, Zhaoqi Xiao, Zhun Wang, Hongwei Li, Wenbo Guo, Heng Yin, Zhenkai Liang, Dawn Song
  • Subjects: cs.CR, cs.AI, cs.LG
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10723
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.10723
  • Copy Paste: [[2611.10723]] CyberBinGym: Can AI Agents Turn Binary Patches into Real Exploits?(https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10723)
  • Keywords: security, attack
  • Abstract: Security fixes usually reach users as compiled updates, but deployment delays leave unpatched systems exposed. During this window, comparing patched and unpatched binaries can reveal how to exploit a vulnerability, turning a defensive update into actionable information for attackers. Existing benchmarks do not measure this capability: they expose source code, rely on human-crafted challenges, or evaluate binary comprehension without requiring exploitation. We introduce CyberBinGym, a benchmark containing 100 tasks derived from real vulnerabilities in 53 widely used projects, spanning six vulnerability families and three levels of analysis complexity. Agents receive stripped vulnerable and patched binaries without source code or vulnerability information, and must combine static and dynamic analysis to recover and exploit the vulnerability. Evaluation separately measures vulnerability recovery through differential crash testing and end-to-end exploitation through flag retrieval, with trajectory audits to reject reward hacking. The strongest evaluated agent recovers the target vulnerability in 88% of tasks and exploits it in 20%. Providing the patched binary raises its exploit rate from 10% to 25% on a matched 20-task subset; with standard binary hardening enabled, it still exploits two of its 20 previously successful tasks. These results demonstrate that current agents can turn released binary patches into working exploits in a controlled setting, although exploitation remains unreliable. These capabilities lower the barriers to targeting unpatched software and compress defenders' effective response window, increasing the urgency of timely patch deployment.

  • Keywords: biometric
  • Abstract: Handedness is a soft-biometric cue for forensic document examination and writer profiling, yet reported accuracies from offline handwriting rarely keep writers, texts and image acquisition apart, so models may recognise the writer or capture conditions rather than the writing hand. Such controlled evidence is missing for Bangla. Using our BanglaHand dataset of 42 writers (21 left- and 21 right-handed), who each wrote six shared prompts and two own texts under controlled collection and photography, we evaluate handedness identification with all images of each test writer held out, whole-writer permutation tests and photometric controls.   • Abstract: Aligning Large Language Models (LLMs) with human preferences via Reinforcement Learning from Human Feedback (RLHF) has driven substantial improvements across a wide range of tasks, but the cost of acquiring preference labels remains the dominant bottleneck in the alignment pipeline. While many recent alignment methods improve sample efficiency through active exploration over candidate responses, they typically query the oracle uniformly across contexts, even for contexts where the learned reward estimates are already reliable enough to provide synthetic labels. We propose Adaptive Ensemble-disagreement Routing for Oracle feedback (AERO), a fully online RLHF algorithm built on a Dyna-style model-based RL architecture, in which an ensemble-based Epistemic Reward Model (ERM) serves both as a reward posterior for exploration and as a learned preference-feedback model for synthetic labeling. Title: Mirage: Provable Defense Against LLM KV Cache Timing Side Channels with Topology-Aware Isolation

  • Authors: Xiaobei Yan, Tianwei Zhang
  • Subjects: cs.CR, cs.AI
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10756
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.10756
  • Copy Paste: [[2611.10756]] Mirage: Provable Defense Against LLM KV Cache Timing Side Channels with Topology-Aware Isolation(https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10756)
  • Keywords: privacy, defense, attack, large language model
  • Abstract: Large Language Models (LLMs) are increasingly deployed as shared multi-tenant services, relying on advanced optimizations like KV cache and prefix sharing to improve throughput and reduce latency. However, these optimizations introduce a critical vulnerability: attackers can exploit time-to-first-token (TTFT) variations between KV cache hits and misses to infer sensitive prompt tokens via prefix-matching side-channel attacks. Existing defenses indiscriminately restrict cross-tenant cache sharing, which incurs prohibitive performance overhead and negates the fundamental efficiency benefits of the KV cache. We propose Mirage, a topology-aware emission obfuscation framework that provably bounds timing leakage without sacrificing the performance gains of cache sharing. Instead of stalling the GPU or forcing redundant recomputations, Mirage delays token emission at the gateway so the attacker observes a natural cache miss distribution, while the GPU seamlessly continues serving other requests at full speed. Mirage uses a Privacy Odometer that persistently tracks the topological novelty of prefix fan-out expansions to distinguish aggressive probing from benign traffic. Once a prefix exceeds a safety threshold, Mirage engages a two-phase policy that uses a dynamic estimator to pad the TTFT to a calibrated cache-miss baseline. On a 500-sample public corpus across three models, Mirage suppresses the timing side-channel to near the random-guessing floor, while maintaining a significantly lower TTFT than defense baselines. We further show that Mirage withstands a strong adaptive adversary and the same principle carries over to semantic caching layers.

Title: ConwayNet: Interpretability, Activation Coherence, and Noise Resilience via a Game-of-Life-Inspired Lateral Inhibition in Artificial Neural Networks

  • Authors: Yair Cohen, Scott M. Gilmore
  • Subjects: cs.LG
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10760
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.10760
  • Copy Paste: [[2611.10760]] ConwayNet: Interpretability, Activation Coherence, and Noise Resilience via a Game-of-Life-Inspired Lateral Inhibition in Artificial Neural Networks(https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10760)
  • Keywords: interpretability
  • Abstract: Deep neural networks achieve universal function approximation by stacking interconnected nonlinearities, but as a consequence they are sensitive to noise and resist mechanistic interpretation. In contrast, the local update rules of cellular automata, such as Conway's Game of Life, give rise to not merely interpretable but programmable dynamics. We explore a bridge between these worlds: a lateral inhibition in neural networks based on continuous Game-of-Life rules that derives inhibitory and excitatory responses from the layer input and blends them into the activation map in a state-conditioned way. The mechanism is differentiable and architecture-agnostic, and its regulation is imposed rather than learned: the rule's parameters are fixed and are not optimised, while the rule's values and derivatives shape training throughout the network. Wired into fully connected, convolutional, wide-residual and graph convolutional networks, it gives rise to several properties. Activation maps become coherent across space, depth and seeds, with coarse structure persisting through depth while fine detail is renewed at every layer. The co-activation graph of the hidden units departs from an Erdős--Rényi random graph: it is laid out on the image and its giant component grows in steps, as in a spatial graph. Networks started from nearly identical initialisations, under label noise, disagree on thirty times fewer test images. Saliency maps rest on the object rather than the background, and resilience to pixel noise not seen in training is substantially improved.

Experiments on four Med-FMs and six chest X-ray and histopathology datasets show that backdoors transfer across models, but asymmetrically and in a dataset-dependent manner. Compared with four training-free input transformations, DePLe reduces backdoor accuracy more consistently while preserving, and in several settings improving, clean accuracy. Title: Zeroth-Order Riemannian Optimization on Fixed-Rank Manifolds for LLM Fine-Tuning

  • Authors: Xinjue Wang, Xiuheng Wang, Yejun Zhang, Sergiy A. Vorobyov, Esa Ollila
  • Subjects: cs.LG, math.OC
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10792
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.10792
  • Copy Paste: [[2611.10792]] Zeroth-Order Riemannian Optimization on Fixed-Rank Manifolds for LLM Fine-Tuning(https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10792)
  • Keywords: large language model
  • Abstract: Zeroth-order (ZO) optimization estimates descent directions from function values alone, which removes the activation storage required by backpropagation and makes it attractive for memory-efficient fine-tuning of large language models (LLMs). Existing low-rank ZO methods either parameterize the additive update through Euclidean factors with a non-unique representation, or restrict each finite-difference direction to be low-rank. In this work, we propose to constrain each additive update block to a fixed-rank matrix manifold while keeping the pretrained weights frozen, so the learned update remains rank-constrained throughout fine-tuning. On this geometry, we build a Riemannian ZO gradient estimator that projects a random matrix onto the tangent space, normalizes it to correct the scale mismatch induced by projection, and further propose Zeroth-order Riemannian Gradient Descent (ZO-RGD) and its smoothed variant, ZO-RGD-S. We show that the ambient finite difference matches its retracted counterpart, the normalized estimator recovers the Riemannian gradient in expectation, and ZO-RGD converges under trajectory-regularity conditions. Experiments on LLM fine-tuning tasks show that the proposed algorithms are competitive with strong forward-only baselines across model families and tasks, with gains in several settings.

Title: Agents of Chaos: A Systematization of Knowledge (SoK) on Malicious Attacks on Agentic AI Systems

  • Abstract: Agentic AI systems extend large language models with tools, memory, retrieval, browser or computer-use interfaces, code execution, external services, and multi-agent coordination. We identify distinct class-specific activation patterns and separation between insertion and later activation. Safeguard coverage remains uneven: only 278 of 660 relationships are directly assessed, and strong coverage is uncommon. These findings support lifecycle-aware threat modeling, agent evaluation, tool governance, and defense-in-depth security for agentic AI systems.

  • Abstract: Image fingerprinting assigns each distributed copy a user-specific watermark for source tracing. Averaging collusion is a central threat: several recipients can average their differently marked copies to suppress each individual mark. Discrete bit-string watermarks are especially vulnerable because averaging drives bit evidence toward ambiguous decisions. We introduce \textbf{VectorMark}, which maps user keys to near-orthogonal high-dimensional unit vectors and identifies colluders by cosine similarity to the extracted watermark direction. Under ideal averaging of $K$ vector keys, the normalized average preserves a cosine signal of order $1/\sqrt{K}$ for each colluder, while unrelated keys remain concentrated near zero. The spherical geometry of random directions gives a shifted-Beta null distribution for cosine similarities, enabling analytic per-key false-positive-rate control after validation on unwatermarked images. VectorMark uses a neural watermark encoder and centered extractor trained with JND masking and a progressive curriculum for photometric, geometric, and collusion robustness. Experiments on MS-COCO, OpenImages, and AI-generated images show that VectorMark maintains robust watermark attribution under photometric and geometric distortions, and achieves the best strict all-colluder detection among evaluated methods, while preserving high visual quality.   • Abstract: Learning predictive models for dynamics is difficult because observations are often sparse and noisy, while the underlying dynamics evolve on nonlinear configuration manifolds and are affected by environmental dissipation and stochastic disturbances. The implicit-explicit integrator additionally provides unconditional stability for the frozen linear damping subsystem. The resulting model learns predictive dynamics that are robust to noise, respect rigid body geometry, and capture both intrinsic motion and environmental dissipation. Experiments on various noisy rigid body benchmarks show improved prediction accuracy, stronger long-horizon stability, and better satisfaction of manifold constraints compared with neural dynamics baselines. Title: BRAT: Bounded Residual-Aligned Training for Stable Multi-Pass LLM Post-Training

  • Authors: Xunlan Zhou, Yuzhe Zhao, Xuhong Huang, Feng Chen, Xuanlin Chen, Lang Feng, Bo An
  • Subjects: cs.LG
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10873
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.10873
  • Copy Paste: [[2611.10873]] BRAT: Bounded Residual-Aligned Training for Stable Multi-Pass LLM Post-Training(https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10873)
  • Keywords: large language model
  • Abstract: Reinforcement learning for large language model post-training often reuses each batch of sampled rollouts for several gradient passes to amortize the cost of generation. Under such multi-pass reuse, existing objectives fail in different ways: unclipped updates collapse as the policy drifts away from the sampling policy, adding a negative baseline avoids collapse but reinforces every sampled response, and PPO-style ratio clipping cuts off gradients abruptly. We first ask why reuse is fragile. Measuring how well an update estimated from a small rollout group aligns with the KL-regularized target on held-out rollouts of the same prompts, we find that this alignment drops by nearly an order of magnitude from in-sample to held-out rollouts across three base models, suggesting that repeated passes largely overfit the sampled group. Motivated by this finding, we propose Bounded Residual-Aligned Training (BRAT), motivated by a proximal update toward a bounded reweighting of the sampling policy. BRAT assigns each token a gradient weight that decreases linearly with its probability ratio and vanishes at a trust-region boundary, and normalizes group-centered rewards by their maximum magnitude, keeping advantages zero-mean and bounded without extra hyperparameters. With four reuse passes, BRAT avoids training collapse, limits policy drift, and achieves higher average accuracy with lower seed variance on most benchmarks.

Title: Optimal Byzantine-resilient Federated Learning with User-level Differential Privacy

  • Authors: Ming Xiang, Stratis Ioannidis, Edmund Yeh, Carlee Joe-Wong, Lili Su
  • Subjects: cs.LG, cs.DC, math.OC
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10898
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.10898
  • Copy Paste: [[2611.10898]] Optimal Byzantine-resilient Federated Learning with User-level Differential Privacy(https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10898)
  • Keywords: privacy, protect, robust, federate
  • Abstract: Real-world deployment of federated learning (FL) requires resilience against adversarial clients and data privacy. Byzantine fault captures the worst-case behavior of adversarial clients, while differential privacy (DP) provides statistical guarantees against privacy loss. In particular, user-level DP guards a client's entire contribution and provides more realistic protection against information leakage. Despite intensive efforts, the minimax-optimal convergence bound of Byzantine-resilient FL with user-level DP remains open. We establish an information-theoretic lower bound for any Byzantine-resilient empirical risk minimization problem under user-level DP and heterogeneous clients. We propose ByzULDP, a central DP algorithm featuring a two-level momentum across the client and server. Client-side momentum mitigates stochastic gradient variance during robust aggregation. Unlike the fault-free case, naively combining non-linear Byzantine-resilient aggregation with central DP noise can be insufficient to guarantee convergence to a bounded neighborhood of a stationary point. Our key contribution is a server-side momentum that leverages a property specific to user-level DP, where privacy sensitivity depends on how client updates are constructed, to empower convergence. ByzULDP achieves the order-optimal convergence bound for strongly-convex objectives, with vanishing error terms decaying at the optimal $O(1/T)$ and $O(1/\sqrt{T})$ rates for strongly-convex and non-convex objectives, respectively. We corroborate our analysis with numerical experiments on real-world datasets across diverse privacy budgets.

Title: Prescriptive Learning of Linear Policies with Feasibility Guarantees for Binary Decision-Making

  • Abstract: Binary decision-making under uncertainty arises across a wide range of applications, including energy systems scheduling, transportation, and workforce planning. We demonstrate the framework on two case studies, namely a unit commitment problem and a staff allocation problem. Out-of-sample experiments under test distributions that either match or differ from the training distribution show that the robust model is more conservative when the two distributions coincide, while under distributional shift it substantially reduces unmet demand and lowers total cost by up to 76.5% relative to classical predictive learning.

This needs 22x fewer candidate evaluations in the generation step and 600x fewer for off-the-shelf generations, relative to brute-force search. Beyond transformation discovery, we analyze the reliability of the reduced-budget evaluation commonly used for augmentation search. Correlating one-epoch selection scores with 20-epoch outcomes reveals strong, weak, and absent predictive relationships depending on the dataset and architecture, with correlations ranging from 0.756 to near zero. A positive control indicates that range restriction alone is unlikely to explain the low correlations. Our results indicate that semantic synthesis with code LLMs provides an efficient alternative to search-based transformation discovery and highlight the importance of validating evaluation fidelity when optimizing data transformations.

Title: CoupledFlow: One-Step Neural Operators for Coupled Multi-Physics PDEs

  • Authors: Trong Khiem Tran, Minh Long Bui, Phi Le Nguyen, Mrinal Sen, Sanjay Srinivasan, Trong Nghia Hoang, Jana Doppa
  • Subjects: cs.LG, cs.AI
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10945
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.10945
  • Copy Paste: [[2611.10945]] CoupledFlow: One-Step Neural Operators for Coupled Multi-Physics PDEs(https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10945)
  • Keywords: generative
  • Abstract: This paper introduces COUPLED FLOW, a new neural operator learning framework for multi-physics PDE systems that captures the co-evolution of multiple physical processes by modeling their cross-process interactions. Unlike existing neural operator methods that primarily target single-physics settings, COUPLED FLOW employs a novel structure-constrained flow matching algorithm to learn process- specific mean velocity fields conditioned on the full multi-physics state. This enables fast and accurate one-step prediction of coupled PDE systems that would otherwise require expensive numerical simulations. A key challenge in training COUPLED FLOW however lies in sampling structure-constrained conditional flow interpolants that capture cross-process correlations and serve as training examples for flow matching. To address this challenge, we derive a multi-physics mean-flow consistency identity that characterizes cross-process interactions and defines the structure-constrained mean-flow matching loss. We also establish a theoretical re- sult showing that the Wasserstein-2 distance between the predicted and oracle PDE solution distributions is upper-bounded in terms of the structure-constrained flow matching loss and the smoothness of the learned flow. Experiments across diverse coupled PDE benchmarks demonstrate that COUPLED FLOW consistently outperforms existing state-of-the-art neural operators and generative PDE solvers.

Title: Validating AI-Discovered Cloud Threats on Live Infrastructure: A Multi-Model, Multi-Cloud Study

  • Authors: Nicholas Pecka, Lotfi Ben Othmane, Renee Bryce
  • Subjects: cs.CR
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10947
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.10947
  • Copy Paste: [[2611.10947]] Validating AI-Discovered Cloud Threats on Live Infrastructure: A Multi-Model, Multi-Cloud Study(https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10947)
  • Keywords: security
  • Abstract: Cloud infrastructure threat modeling lacks empirical validation of AI-discovered threats. AI-assisted discovery is a practical input to security workflows when paired with empirical validation.

  • Authors: Binchi Zhang, Hadi Abdullah, Yiwei Cai, Jundong Li
  • Subjects: cs.CL
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10971
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.10971

  • Abstract: Safety-aligned large language models often lose their safety behaviors after fine-tuning, even when safety data are included. In this paper, we propose that formulaic refusal strings can function as outlier modes: narrow, weakly grounded response patterns that are easy to fit but vulnerable to subsequent updates. Controlled experiments support this account: fixed refusals are acquired much like arbitrary constant strings, in contrast to prompt-grounded natural responses. Across multiple models and alignment regimes, natural safety targets improve both immediate safety and its retention under continued fine-tuning without sacrificing helpfulness. These findings suggest a practical principle for more durable safety alignment: construct safety targets that draw on the model's existing language capabilities and remain grounded in the meaning of each request.

  • Subjects: cs.CL, cs.LG

  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.10974

  • Keywords: robust, extraction, interpretability, large language model
  • Abstract: Reasoning in large language models can be disrupted by simple input perturbations (e.g. adding irrelevant distractors), suggesting reliance on spurious correlations. Identifying and suppressing the spurious features involved in long chain-of-thought reasoning remains challenging. We introduce Spurious-feature WEight EdiTing (SWEET), a training-free method that combines counterfactual interventions with mechanistic interpretability to identify and suppress spurious-features. SWEET extracts token- and sequence-level behavioral directions from activation contrasts between original problems and their counterfactual variants - capturing local and global patterns of spurious reasoning. It also reduces the relative gap between original and perturbed accuracies from 17.6% to 2.5%. Analyses identify two recurring forms of spurious reasoning, i.e. over-integrating irrelevant conditions and overthinking, and provide mechanistic evidence that SWEET improves reasoning robustness by suppressing both behavioral directions.

Title: Horizon-Scalable Q-Learning

  • Authors: Haoran Xu, Pragya Srivastava, Xinjie Liu, Siddhant Agarwal, Amy Zhang
  • Subjects: cs.LG, cs.AI
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10987
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.10987
  • Copy Paste: [[2611.10987]] Horizon-Scalable Q-Learning(https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10987)
  • Keywords: diffusion
  • Abstract: Learning accurate off-policy state-action value functions (Q-functions) from offline datasets becomes harder as the task horizon grows. One-step temporal-difference (TD) learning bootstraps after every transition, so small per-step errors accumulate along the horizon, and early states can be badly misvalued even when the TD loss is small. Multi-step TD shortens this chain by following the dataset trajectory for several steps; however, since the behavior policy is often suboptimal, every followed action adds a bias, and no fixed number of steps suits a whole trajectory. In this paper, we propose Horizon-Scalable Q-Learning (HQL), a simple method that enables accurate long-horizon value learning by automatically balancing between one-step and multi-step returns. HQL extends a target through a dataset action only when the critic values it at least as highly as the best-of-N actions sampled from the BC policy, and otherwise falls back to a one-step target. We show that this rule is a principled off-policy correction that needs no action densities, which makes HQL compatible with expressive flow and diffusion policies. Experiments show that HQL avoids the failures of both one-step and multi-step TD on a combination-lock task across horizons, and outperforms prior horizon-reduction methods on long-horizon OGBench and OCBench tasks.

Title: DPDP: Diffusion Policy-Steered Diffusion Planner for Offline Reinforcement Learning

  • Authors: Ruyi An, Mingyuan Zhou
  • Subjects: cs.LG
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10993
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.10993
  • Copy Paste: [[2611.10993]] DPDP: Diffusion Policy-Steered Diffusion Planner for Offline Reinforcement Learning(https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10993)
  • Keywords: diffusion
  • Abstract: In offline reinforcement learning, diffusion models fill two roles with complementary strengths. Typically guided by a critic, a diffusion policy is value-aware and favors high-return, behavior-supported actions, but it is short-sighted: it never lays out the states it intends to visit. A diffusion planner is far-sighted, synthesizing such a long-horizon trajectory, but it is only behavior-driven at execution, realizing each planned transition using only offline this http URL is strong exactly where the other is weak. We introduce DPDP to bring them together at the planner's execution step, where a planned transition becomes an action. Reading the planner's inverse-dynamics executor as a posterior over actions: the environment's transition likelihood relates an action to the planned next state, while the behavioral prior determines its weight under the offline data. Replacing this prior with a value-optimized diffusion policy preserves the planned transition likelihood and favors valuable actions that can serve the plan. We target the resulting clean-action distribution in two ways. A training-free sampler composes policy and inverse-dynamics scores at matched action noise levels and applies Langevin correction; this route uses a shared action noise schedule and an approximately common terminal prior. A preference objective instead amortizes the transfer into a single diffusion executor. Across D4RL, DPDP improves the planner it builds on, staying competitive with reactive diffusion policies on locomotion and leading on long-horizon navigation.

Title: Toward a theory of Evaluability

  • Authors: Kunal Talwar
  • Subjects: cs.LG, stat.ML
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10996

  • Keywords: fair
  • Abstract: Machine learning models, particularly large foundation models, are increasingly evaluated along multiple axes that are relevant for their usage. In addition to accuracy measures appropriate for the specific task of interest, measures capturing safety, calibration and fairness properties are often of interest. The classical approach to these problems assumes that the test set has been held out from the training process, an assumption that is increasingly harder to justify for large foundation models. In this work, we formalize a notion of evaluability without this assumption, and ask if it is feasible to evaluate black-box models in settings where our test samples may have been used during training. We study this problem under different settings, capturing different levels of access to the model. We fully characterize the class of distributions where accurate evaluation is possible, and show tight upper and lower bounds on the sample complexity of evaluation. Our results show that black-box evaluation in this setup is feasible if and only if the distribution is close to being small support. We then consider evaluation algorithms that can query the model on additional inputs and demonstrate connections to self-correctors studied in program testing. Using these, we show that a small amount of query access can increase the power of the evaluator for natural distributions and concept classes.

  • Subjects: cs.LG, cs.AI
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11005
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.11005
  • Copy Paste: [[2611.11005]] GradReason: LLMs as Meta-Learners that Replace Training Epochs with Training-Data-Free Weight Updates(https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11005)
  • Keywords: data-free, large language model
  • Abstract: Standard training recipes make a fixed number of passes over the training data, even when an epoch mostly adds mini-batch noise. We study whether a large language model (LLM) that reads the weight and gradient history of a run can replace some of these epochs with updates that compute no gradient and read no training image. In GradReason, an LLM agent is called every ten epochs. In a restricted sandbox, it analyses recent weight snapshots, epoch-mean gradients, and validation metrics, then submits a Python program that computes new weights and a plan marking each of the next ten epochs as data or virtual. A virtual epoch is kept only if it passes a forward-only check on 5,000 held-out validation images. In 120 runs on CIFAR-10 and CIFAR-100 with three architectures, Claude Opus 5.5 used 32.8% fewer data epochs than the 80-epoch recipe at 0.31 percentage points lower test accuracy (95% CI -0.72 to +0.09; 0.94 points on ResNet-18), on the recipe's own frontier of data against accuracy. Unprompted, Opus averaged recent weight snapshots in every run. This choice produced the savings: a fixed rule with the same acceptance check but extrapolations from prior work saved 5.4% of the data epochs, and the same rule with averaging added saved 25.0%. The LLM's contribution is to find a working update within a single run; once found, a fixed rule implements it equally well. An Opus run cost 0.68 USD, but at this scale the agent's latency exceeded the GPU time it saved.

  • Keywords: privacy, large language model
  • Abstract: Embodied intelligence systems rely on fast and reliable inference to enable real-time perception, reasoning, and control. Recent advances in large-scale artificial intelligence (AI) models, including Large Language Models (LLMs) and Vision-Language-Action (VLA) models, have significantly enhanced the capabilities of embodied agents in complex environments. However, deploying these models remains challenging due to the limited computational resources and energy budgets of robotic platforms. A fundamental question is where inference should be executed. Collaborative inference, which partitions models across end devices, edge servers, and the cloud, has emerged as a promising solution. Yet determining the optimal partitioning point remains an open problem, as it depends on wireless network conditions, model structure, and privacy requirements. This article reviews the state of the art in collaborative inference for embodied intelligence and proposes an edge-centric framework in which the edge coordinates model partitioning and execution. Through both simulation and real-world experiments over WiFi and 5G networks, we demonstrate that the optimal partitioning strategy varies with network conditions and model structure. We further identify latency-minimizing partition points for ResNet-101, Qwen2.5-VL-7B, and OpenVLA-7B in collaborative inference between NVIDIA Jetson Nano or AGX Xavier devices and an AGX Thor edge server under varying wireless conditions. Finally, we discuss the future directions for collaborative inference in 6G-empowered embodied intelligence systems.

Title: MoTo: Mixture of Tokenizers Towards Fair Multilingual Language Modeling

  • Authors: Gül Sena Altıntaş, Colin Raffel
  • Subjects: cs.CL, cs.LG
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11027
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.11027
  • Copy Paste: [[2611.11027]] MoTo: Mixture of Tokenizers Towards Fair Multilingual Language Modeling(https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11027)
  • Keywords: fair, large language model
  • Abstract: Large language models rely on a single tokenizer that is chosen at training time and fixed thereafter. Most tokenizers are trained on English-dominant corpora, making them under-serve morphologically rich and non-Latin-script languages, where they can produce fragmented representations, longer sequence lengths, and reduced information density during training. We propose Mixture of Tokenizers MoTo, a modular tokenization framework that trains dedicated per-language BPE tokenizers and composes them into a unified superset vocabulary, where tokens shared across languages are deduplicated into a common ID space. Each tokenizer maintains its own normalization policy, pre-tokenization rules, and vocabulary budget. Despite allocating significantly fewer entries to English, MoTo retains English performance while delivering consistent gains across 18 out of 21 typologically diverse languages spanning six scripts, with improvements most pronounced on morphologically complex and non-Latin-script languages that are systematically underserved by English-dominant tokenizer design. Our results suggest that modular subword tokenization is a practical and extensible alternative to monolithic tokenization for multilingual parity.

Title: Building and Auditing Chemical Datasets with LLMs: A General LLM-as-a-Judge Framework Applied to Homogeneous Catalytic PET Depolymerisation

  • Authors: Muhammad bin Javaid, Karim K. Ben Hicham, Enkhnyam Battulga, Alexander Mitsos, Martin Grohe
  • Subjects: cs.CL, cs.AI, cs.DL
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11030
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.11030
  • Copy Paste: [[2611.11030]] Building and Auditing Chemical Datasets with LLMs: A General LLM-as-a-Judge Framework Applied to Homogeneous Catalytic PET Depolymerisation(https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11030)
  • Keywords: extraction, large language model
  • Abstract: Large language models can rapidly extract structured multivariate experimental data from the chemistry literature, yet verifying thousands of generated records without ground truth remains difficult. We release the extracted dataset with the complete LLM judge audit logs, as well as the Chemistry Data Extractor Toolkit, an open-source application implementing this end-to-end pipeline in a code-free, browser-based environment for document parsing, customizable schema and prompt design, model-agnostic extraction and auditing, interactive provenance-highlighted review, and reproducible dataset bundling.

Title: Virtual Task Prompting for Multi-Task Scene Understanding

  • Authors: Zedong Wang, Yucheng Wang, Dan Xu
  • Subjects: cs.CV

  • Abstract: Multi-task scene understanding models typically rely on modified vision encoders or complex decoders for cross-task interaction, which limits their flexibility and compatibility with off-the-shelf vision foundation models (VFMs). Title: Differentiable Virtual-Slot Partitioning for Adaptive Tokenization in Vision Transformers

  • Authors: Gaoxiang He, Bin Zhu, Bo Xie, Xiaotong Yu, Yi Chen, Hongfei Yan
  • Subjects: cs.CV
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11058
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.11058
  • Copy Paste: [[2611.11058]] Differentiable Virtual-Slot Partitioning for Adaptive Tokenization in Vision Transformers(https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11058)
  • Keywords: transformer
  • Abstract: Adaptive tokenization aims to reduce the compute spent on uninformative image regions while preserving accuracy. Existing work relies on (i) fixed or frozen scoring rules, which cannot be trained toward the task loss, or (ii) discrete masks trained with straight-through estimators, which yield noisy gradients, while fully differentiable alternatives instead leave the pre-trained regular grid. We revisit adaptive tokenization from a regular-grid perspective and ask: can a two-scale quadtree be learned end-to-end without deleting a single token during training? We show that one coarse token and its four fine children can be made the two endpoints of a single continuous interpolation. We introduce Differentiable Virtual-Slot Partitioning (\dvsp{}), which keeps four \emph{virtual slots} per $2{\times}2$ region and blends their token features and attention mass with a single weight $\alpha$, exactly reproducing the discrete grid at $\alpha \in \{0,1\}$. On COCO detection with EVA-02-B, \dvsp{} retains $97.9\%$ of dense Box AP while removing $64.2\%$ of visual tokens, giving a $1.61\times$ end-to-end speedup, and it outperforms entropy-based APT, DART, and uniform lower-resolution dense adaptation at matched budgets; these gains depend on the conserved attention mass, whose removal costs $1.59$ Box AP. On a frozen Segmenter it also improves Cityscapes mIoU, and on frozen DeiT backbones it exceeds entropy selection at identical per-image token counts.

Title: Beyond AUROC: Deployment-Aware Evaluation of Model-Internal Jailbreak Detectors

  • Authors: Mohammad Tehabsim, Basil Al-Housani, Nour Alhussien, Phung Lai
  • Subjects: cs.CR, cs.CL
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11067
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.11067
  • Copy Paste: [[2611.11067]] Beyond AUROC: Deployment-Aware Evaluation of Model-Internal Jailbreak Detectors(https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11067)
  • Keywords: security, defense, attack, large language model
  • Abstract: Large language models (LLMs) are increasingly deployed in user-facing systems, where jailbreak attacks can bypass safety controls and induce harmful and policy-violating outputs. Recent model-internal detectors use hidden representations, gradients, or internal signals to identify such prompts, but their practical security value depends critically on behavior at low false-positive operating points rather than on area under the receiver operating characteristic curve (AUROC) alone. We study two data dependencies that shape this behavior: calibration data, which determine the detection threshold, and reference data, which can influence detector scores. Across four model-internal detectors, four LLMs, and twelve jailbreak families, thresholds calibrated on curated benign prompts frequently fail to preserve their false-positive rate (FPR) budgets on real-user traffic. Recalibration improves false-positive control but reveals substantial family-level coverage gaps, including cases with high AUROC but low true-positive rate (TPR). Causal reference-set interventions show that changing reference composition can substantially alter family-level detection and that improving coverage of one attack family can reduce coverage of others. These findings show that calibration and reference data are part of a detector's security behavior. We therefore recommend evaluating model-internal jailbreak defenses using realized FPR on deployment-like traffic and per-family TPR at explicit low-FPR operating points, with AUROC used as a complementary ranking metric rather than the primary security measure.

Title: Discrete Diffusion Playground: A 2D Benchmark for Discrete Generative Models

  • Authors: Justin Shao, Sihyun Park, David Ryan Koes, Maria Chikina
  • Subjects: cs.LG
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11068
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.11068

  • Keywords: diffusion, generative
  • Abstract: Discrete diffusion is a promising framework for categorical generative modeling, but current evaluation protocols often fail to reveal whether models have actually learned the target distribution. We introduce the Discrete Diffusion Playground, a diagnostic benchmark that converts known 2D continuous distributions into binary token sequences through an exact discretized bijection. This creates a rare setting in which models are trained on discrete sequences, but generated samples can be decoded back into 2D and compared directly against a known ground-truth distribution. The benchmark spans smooth Gaussian mixtures and tilted checkerboards with sharp boundaries, disconnected support, and coordinate-dependent structure, allowing it to expose failures that are invisible in standard proxy metrics. It supports exact grid divergences, Wasserstein distances, density visualizations, and nonparametric two-sample tests, providing both quantitative evaluation and interpretable failure diagnosis. Using this testbed, we show that masked diffusion models can learn meaningful adaptive reveal orders but remain less stable than autoregressive baselines on smooth targets and largely fail on tilted checkerboards, where autoregressive models preserve the target geometry. Control experiments with reversed and interleaved token orderings show that autoregressive performance is not explained by favorable bit order alone. The Playground provides a simple but stringent benchmark for stress-testing discrete generative models and for developing evaluation metrics that detect real distributional mismatch rather than proxy performance. Our code is available at this https URL .

Title: System Prompts Affect Response Bias More Than Discriminability in LLM Safety

  • Authors: Mohan W. Gupta, Sandy Tanwisuth
  • Subjects: cs.LG, cs.AI, cs.CL, cs.SE
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11074
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.11074
  • Copy Paste: [[2611.11074]] System Prompts Affect Response Bias More Than Discriminability in LLM Safety(https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11074)
  • Keywords: large language model
  • Abstract: Large language model safety evaluations often focus on a compliance-refusal tradeoff: models should refuse unsafe requests without refusing safe ones. We argue that this misses a broader form of prompt-conditioned task selection, where the same request can elicit different behaviors depending on the system prompt. We investigate this using a controlled evaluation crossing eight safety-task prompt families with three clarity levels across HarmBench, XSTest, and IHEval. Holding benchmark items fixed, we evaluate 13 models and use signal detection theory to distinguish two possibilities: whether models become better at telling safe from unsafe requests (discriminability), or simply more willing to refuse (response criterion). We find substantial differences in discriminability across models, but system prompts primarily shift the willingness to refuse, with smaller and less consistent effects on discriminability. Increasing prompt clarity makes models more willing to refuse without reliably improving their ability to distinguish safe from unsafe requests. Beyond refusal, prompt families also shift responses among six distinct behavioral policies, including clarification and safe help. Together, these findings show that system prompts primarily change how models respond to safety-relevant requests rather than consistently improving their ability to distinguish safe from unsafe ones.

  • Abstract: Multi-stage large language model (LLM) cascades balance accuracy and inference cost, but reliable deployment also requires controlling error risk and identifying its sources across stages.   • Subjects: cs.LG
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11170

Title: Source-Anchored Visual Autoregression for Extreme Image Compression

  • Authors: Bingxin Wang, Yuan Lan, Yang Xiang, Yiqing Huang
  • Subjects: cs.CV
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11200
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.11200
  • Copy Paste: [[2611.11200]] Source-Anchored Visual Autoregression for Extreme Image Compression(https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11200)
  • Keywords: generative
  • Abstract: In the extreme-low bitrate range below $0.01$ bpp, image compression becomes a constrained generation problem: the reconstruction depends on inferring plausible missing details while staying close to the input image. Autoregressive token models provide an efficient coarse-to-fine prior for this setting, but the transmitted early-scale tokens often leave object identity and texture underdetermined, which can lead to source-inconsistent reconstructions. We introduce Source-Anchored Visual Autoregression (SAVAR), a generative codec built on Visual Autoregressive (VAR) modeling. SAVAR transmits early-scale tokens and a compact caption. The tokens provide image-specific layout information, while the caption supplies global semantics. SAVAR reconstructs a partial VQ-VAE latent from the retained scales, maps it to the start-of-sequence initialization, and uses the same latent to condition the caption embedding before adaptive layer normalization, separating two roles that are typically driven by a shared condition in VAR-style generation. On the Kodak and CLIC2020 datasets in the extreme-low range, SAVAR reduces perceptual distortion relative to matched ARPC retained-scale operating points, lowering LPIPS by about $8$--$9\%$ and DISTS by up to $22\%$ at a lower bpp. It reconstructs a $512 \times 512$ image in about $218$ ms on one NVIDIA L20X GPU, retaining fast autoregressive decoding in the evaluated setting.

Title: Do Feature Interactions Break Explainability? Provable Explanations for Any-Order Neural Additive Models

  • Authors: Idan Refaeli, Yizhak Yisrael Elboher, Guy Katz, Shahaf Bassan
  • Subjects: cs.LG, cs.AI, cs.CC
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11224
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.11224
  • Copy Paste: [[2611.11224]] Do Feature Interactions Break Explainability? Provable Explanations for Any-Order Neural Additive Models(https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11224)
  • Keywords: explainability
  • Abstract: Post-hoc explanation methods for neural networks are often heuristic and lack provable guarantees. A common alternative to such methods that was studied in recent years is to compute a cardinally-minimal subset of input features that is provably sufficient to determine the model's prediction. For general neural networks, however, finding such explanations is computationally intractable. In contrast, recent work showed that this problem becomes more tractable for a particular family of neural networks, namely neural additive models (NAMs), though standard NAMs are highly restrictive because they exclude feature interactions altogether. In this work, we study the computation of provably minimal and sufficient explanations for neural networks with restricted feature interactions, bridging the gap between fully general neural networks and purely additive models. We first prove that even with constant-order interactions, the problem is $\Sigma_2^P$-hard, implying that worst-case exponential complexity is unavoidable. We then identify two provably tractable settings: one based on sparsity in the interaction structure and another based on a stronger interaction-aware notion of sufficiency. Under either of these settings, we develop algorithms that compute provable explanations for neural networks with any-order interactions while retaining much of the computational efficiency of additive models. Empirically, we show that these explanations are significantly smaller and faster to obtain than those produced by standard algorithms, while being derived from models that achieve much higher accuracy than standard NAMs. Overall, our results help expand the class of neural networks that admit efficient provable explanations, while clarifying the fundamental role of feature interactions in shaping their complexity.

Title: TLRS-DPO: Token-Level Risk Scoring for Better Direct Preference Optimization

  • Authors: Suxin Ji, Hungtao Wan
  • Subjects: cs.LG, cs.AI, cs.CL
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11232
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.11232
  • Copy Paste: [[2611.11232]] TLRS-DPO: Token-Level Risk Scoring for Better Direct Preference Optimization(https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11232)
  • Keywords: robust, large language model
  • Abstract: Direct Preference Optimization (DPO) is a simple and widely used alternative to RLHF for aligning large language models (LLMs), but its sequence-level objective cannot precisely locate or suppress the local patterns that make an otherwise acceptable response unsafe or undesirable. We propose TLRS-DPO (Token-Level Risk Scoring for Better Direct Preference Optimization), which augments DPO with fine-grained token-level control while remaining fully offline and reference-based. TLRS-DPO introduces a token-level robust compensation term that converts the policy-reference log-ratio on rejected responses into localized risk scores, a curriculum-style schedule that gradually increases the strength of this compensation during training, and a risk-adaptive screening mechanism that selectively penalizes only high-risk tokens instead of entire sequences. This design bridges sequence-level preference fitting and token-level behavior control, and can be implemented as a drop-in modification to standard DPO trainers. Empirically, TLRS-DPO improves alignment quality and safety-relevant behavior with minimal impact on helpfulness compared to strong DPO baselines.

Title: A Layer-wise Study of How Style Rewriting Breaks the Calibration of Zero-Shot LLM Fake-News Detectors

  • Authors: Prabhjot Singh, Praney Goyal, Raksha Sharma
  • Subjects: cs.CL, cs.AI
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11239
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.11239
  • Copy Paste: [[2611.11239]] A Layer-wise Study of How Style Rewriting Breaks the Calibration of Zero-Shot LLM Fake-News Detectors(https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11239)
  • Keywords: attack, large language model
  • Abstract: Large language models (LLMs) are increasingly asked to judge whether a news article is true, and their stated confidence is often taken at face value. We test what happens to that confidence when an article's writing style, but not its facts, is manipulated, as in recent \emph{style attacks} on fake-news detectors. Tracing how the prediction and its confidence form layer by layer inside the network, we study three model families (LLaMA-3-8B, Qwen2.5-7B, Mistral-7B) on three fake-news benchmarks, and find the same three patterns in every model--dataset pairing. First, on the original articles the models are competent and largely honest judges: they settle on an answer, briefly become overconfident, and then correct their confidence in the final layers. Second, style-rewritten articles break this self-correction: the models become far less accurate, under the strongest attack, worse than random guessing, yet remain just as confident, so they are \emph{confidently wrong} precisely when a warning is most needed. Third, nearly all of the damage comes from rewriting into sensational or emotionally charged styles, while sober, neutral rewrites leave the models' honesty largely intact. The practical implication is that an LLM's confidence about veracity cannot be trusted as a safety signal when an article's style may have been manipulated.

  • Keywords: transformer
  • Abstract: Self-supervised speech models (S3Ms) encode a rich hierarchy of acoustic, prosodic, and phonetic information, yet when and where it emerges during pretraining remains unclear. Title: Chatter Attack: Resource Consumption Attack for Large Language Models

  • Authors: Yibo Miao, Yichuan Cao, Xiao-Shan Gao, Yinpeng Dong
  • Subjects: cs.LG, cs.AI, cs.CR, cs.CV, math.OC
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11261
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.11261
  • Copy Paste: [[2611.11261]] Chatter Attack: Resource Consumption Attack for Large Language Models(https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11261)
  • Keywords: attack, large language model
  • Abstract: LLMs are powerful but incur substantial inference cost, which can be exploited by malicious users to induce overly long outputs, increasing latency and operational expense. Prior resource consumption attacks either coerce repetition or suppress the end-of-sequence token by lowering its logit value. However, with autoregressive, sampling-based generation, the search space grows exponentially with length and small deviations or unstable trajectories can derail optimization, limiting attack effectiveness and stability. To address this issue, we introduce Chatter Attack, a novel class of resource consumption attacks that optimize adversarial prompts to induce predefined short target phrases, thereby avoiding search-space explosion while still eliciting long responses. Specifically, Chatter Attack triggers self-doubt using short doubt-inducing phrases as the target, and sustains this state throughout generation via inference attention loss and global entropy loss. To extend to black-box settings, we further propose a novel Bayesian Prompt Optimization (BPO) method, which models the objective function globally by constructing a discrete token kernel, efficiently exploring the entire search space using prior information. Extensive experiments across multiple models and datasets show that our method outperforms baselines, with maximum response length increases to 31.5$\times$, and revealing practical resource-depletion risks for real-world LLM inference services.

  • Abstract: Reconstructing interactable articulated assets from a few images is key to robotics and simulation, yet highly underconstrained. Video diffusion models capture how objects move between states, but only implicitly in 2D. To this end, we present TransArt3D, an optimization-based framework that reconstructs an articulated 3D asset from two endpoint images by translating this video transition prior into explicit part structure and joint kinematics. Finally, a motion-aware canonical appearance fusion uses the recovered articulation to map both endpoint images onto one canonical surface, texturing regions that only one state reveals.   • Abstract: Quadratic manifold fitting provides a flexible framework for modeling nonlinear low-dimensional structures embedded in high-dimensional spaces. A finding is still resolved by people: it has to reach the people responsible for fixing it and accountable for the resource it concerns. Ownership is the organizational context that decides who that is. Where recorded, it is declared by hand in configuration management databases or owner tags and goes stale unless kept current; accounts are shared, and automation creates much of the infrastructure. The knowledge sits in work records, code history, audit logs and identity directories, encoded in each organization's own conventions. Recovering it poses four challenges: the signal is scattered across sources, none of them authoritative; the conventions that carry it differ between organizations; reaching an owner takes multi-hop links; and those related to a resource differ in the strength of their relation. We define ownership as a set of entities, people or teams, each related to the resource by one of five patterns ordered by strength. We present a reference architecture for agentic ownership attribution that derives ownership from these records instead of declaring it: it profiles an organization offline into tested signal procedures and an ownership playbook, then attributes each resource with a coordinator agent and parallel investigators, with confidence computed by rules. On 100 resources of 50 Amazon Web Services (AWS) resource types in one organization, we pool the architecture's candidates with those of an unprimed general-purpose agent and verify every evidence piece against the records. The architecture reaches 92.1% weighted recall and 86.6% weighted precision, against 54.8% and 77.4%, at 0.55 times the agent's cost per unit of coverage. The difference lies in people who build and change resources without an explicit owner, found through work records and code history.

  • Abstract: CAPTCHAs can protect websites from malicious attacks. According to the experimental results, this method cracked six most popular slide puzzle CAPTCHAs with success rates of over 90\%, and the attack time is only about 5 seconds, indicating that the security of existing slide puzzle CAPTCHAs is facing challenge. At the same time, we propose various methods to enhance the security of slide puzzle CAPTCHAs.

  • Abstract: Diffusion Transformer (DiT) models, while having become a powerful backbone for generative synthesis, suffer from high inference cost on generating high-resolution images.
